    23→| name       | VARCHAR(255)      |                           |
    24→| avatar_url | VARCHAR(512)      |                           |
    25→| role       | ENUM(admin,member)| NOT NULL                  |
    26→| company_id | UUID              | FK → companies._id, NULL  |
    27→| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ       | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()   |
    28→| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ       | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()   |
    29→| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ       | NULL                      |
    30→
    31→- `company_id` is the admin's owned company. NULL for pure members.
    32→
    33→### oauth_accounts
    34→
    35→| Column       | Type                  | Constraints                     |
    36→|--------------|-----------------------|---------------------------------|
    37→| _id          | UUID                  | PK                              |
    38→| user_id      | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL        |
    39→| provider     | ENUM(github,google)   | NOT NULL                        |
    40→| provider_id  | VARCHAR(255)          | NOT NULL                        |
    41→| email        | VARCHAR(255)          |                                 |
    42→| access_token | TEXT                  | Encrypted (AES-256)             |
    43→| created_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |
    44→| updated_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |
    45→| deleted_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                            |
    46→
    47→- UNIQUE(provider, provider_id)
    48→- Supports multiple providers per user (account linking)
    49→
    50→### companies
    51→
    52→| Column     | Type         | Constraints             |
    53→|------------|--------------|-------------------------|
    54→| _id        | UUID         | PK                      |
    55→| name       | VARCHAR(255) | NOT NULL                |
    56→| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    57→| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    58→| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |
    59→
    60→### company_members
    61→
    62→| Column     | Type                  | Constraints                  |
    63→|------------|-----------------------|------------------------------|
    64→| _id        | UUID                  | PK                           |
    65→| user_id    | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL     |
    66→| company_id | UUID                  | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL |
    67→| role       | ENUM(admin,member)    | NOT NULL                     |
    68→| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    69→| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    70→| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                         |
    71→
    72→- UNIQUE(user_id, company_id)
    73→- Admins: 1:1 with company (enforced in app logic)
    74→- Members: can belong to multiple companies
    75→
    76→### invitations
    77→
    78→| Column      | Type                | Constraints                  |
    79→|-------------|---------------------|------------------------------|
    80→| _id         | UUID                | PK                           |
    81→| company_id  | UUID                | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL |
    82→| email       | VARCHAR(255)        | NOT NULL                     |
    83→| role        | ENUM(admin,member)  | NOT NULL                     |
    84→| token       | VARCHAR(255)        | UNIQUE, NOT NULL             |
    85→| expires_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL                     |
    86→| accepted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                         |
    87→| created_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    88→| updated_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    89→| deleted_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                         |
    90→
    91→## Auth Flow
    92→
    93→### OAuth Login Sequence
    94→
    95→1. Frontend redirects to `GET /auth/{provider}`
    96→2. Backend generates state param, redirects to provider
    97→3. Provider redirects back to `GET /auth/{provider}/callback` with code
    98→4. Backend exchanges code for access token via Arctic
    99→5. Backend fetches user profile from provider
   100→6. Account resolution:
   101→   - **Existing oauth_account, same user** → login
   102→   - **Existing oauth_account, different user** → prompt to link
   103→   - **No oauth_account, email matches existing user** → prompt to link
   104→   - **No oauth_account, no email match** → new signup flow
   105→7. Create session in Redis
   106→8. Return JWT (access + refresh) to frontend
   107→
   108→### Token Exchange Security
   109→
   110→- OAuth callback returns a temporary authorization code to frontend (not JWT)
   111→- Frontend exchanges code for JWT via POST — avoids tokens in URL/browser history
   112→
   113→### Token Refresh
   114→
   115→1. Client sends refresh token (from httpOnly cookie)
   116→2. Backend validates refresh token, checks session exists in Redis
   117→3. Issues new access token (1hr), rotates refresh token (30d)
   118→
   119→### Request Authentication
   120→
   121→On every authenticated request:
   122→1. Validate JWT signature + expiry
   123→2. Check session exists in Redis (key: `session:{user_id}:{session_id}`)
   124→3. If session gone → 401, force re-login
   125→
   126→### Logout
   127→
   128→- Delete session key from Redis → JWT becomes invalid immediately
   129→
   130→## Session Structure (Redis)
   131→
   132→```
   133→Key:    session:{user_id}:{session_id}
   134→TTL:    30 days
   135→
   136→Value:  {
   137→  user_id:      "uuid",
   138→  session_id:   "uuid",
   139→  role:         "admin" | "member",
   140→  company_id:   "uuid" | null,
   141→  provider:     "github" | "google",
   142→  ip:           "x.x.x.x",
   143→  user_agent:   "...",
   144→  created_at:   "ISO timestamp",
   145→  last_active:  "ISO timestamp"
   146→}
   147→```
   148→
   149→### Session Operations
   150→
   151→- **Create** — on login, write to Redis with 30d TTL
   152→- **Validate** — on every request, auth guard checks key exists
   153→- **Refresh** — on token refresh, update `last_active`
   154→- **Destroy** — on logout, delete key
   155→- **Destroy all** — scan keys `session:{user_id}:*` and delete (or maintain a set `sessions:{user_id}` for O(1) lookup)
   156→
   157→### Company Context Switching (Members)
   158→
   159→- Member calls `POST /users/me/company` with `company_id`
   160→- Updates `company_id` in Redis session
   161→- Next access token includes new company context
   162→
   163→## API Endpoints
   164→
   165→### OAuth
   166→
   167→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   168→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   169→| GET    | /auth/github               | Redirect to GitHub OAuth       | Public   |
   170→| GET    | /auth/github/callback      | Handle GitHub callback         | Public   |
   171→| GET    | /auth/google               | Redirect to Google OAuth       | Public   |
   172→| GET    | /auth/google/callback      | Handle Google callback         | Public   |
   173→| POST   | /auth/refresh              | Refresh access token           | Cookie   |
   174→| POST   | /auth/logout               | Destroy session                | Auth     |
   175→| POST   | /auth/link                 | Link OAuth to existing user    | Auth     |
   176→
   177→### Signup
   178→
   179→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   180→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   181→| POST   | /auth/signup/company       | Create company after OAuth     | Auth     |
   182→
   183→### User
   184→
   185→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   186→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   187→| GET    | /users/me                  | Current user + active company  | Auth     |
   188→| POST   | /users/me/company          | Switch active company context  | Auth     |
   189→
   190→### Company
   191→
   192→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   193→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   194→| GET    | /companies/:id             | Company details                | Auth     |
   195→
   196→### Invitations (Admin only)
   197→
   198→| Method | Path                              | Description              | Auth     |
   199→|--------|-----------------------------------|--------------------------|----------|
   200→| POST   | /companies/:id/invitations        | Invite engineer by email | Admin    |
   201→| GET    | /companies/:id/invitations        | List pending invitations | Admin    |
   202→| DELETE | /companies/:id/invitations/:invId | Revoke invitation        | Admin    |
   203→| GET    | /invitations/accept?token=xxx     | Accept invite → OAuth    | Public   |
   204→
   205→## Module Architecture
   206→
   207→```
   208→backend/src/modules/
   209→├── auth/
   210→│   ├── auth.module.ts
   211→│   ├── auth.controller.ts
   212→│   ├── auth.service.ts
   213→│   ├── strategies/
   214→│   │   ├── github.strategy.ts      # Arctic GitHub provider
   215→│   │   └── google.strategy.ts      # Arctic Google provider
   216→│   ├── guards/
   217→│   │   ├── auth.guard.ts           # JWT + session validation
   218→│   │   └── roles.guard.ts          # Role-based access
   219→│   ├── decorators/
   220→│   │   ├── current-user.decorator.ts
   221→│   │   ├── roles.decorator.ts
   222→│   │   └── public.decorator.ts
   223→│   ├── dto/
   224→│   ├── types/
   225→│   └── constants/
   226→├── session/
   227→│   ├── session.module.ts
   228→│   ├── session.service.ts
   229→│   └── types/
   230→├── user/
   231→│   ├── user.module.ts
   232→│   ├── user.service.ts
   233→│   ├── user.model.ts
   234→│   ├── dto/
   235→│   └── types/
   236→├── oauth-account/
   237→│   ├── oauth-account.module.ts
   238→│   ├── oauth-account.service.ts
   239→│   ├── oauth-account.model.ts
   240→│   ├── dto/
   241→│   └── types/
   242→├── company/
   243→│   ├── company.module.ts
   244→│   ├── company.service.ts
   245→│   ├── company.model.ts
   246→│   ├── dto/
   247→│   └── types/
   248→├── invitation/
   249→│   ├── invitation.module.ts
   250→│   ├── invitation.service.ts
   251→│   ├── invitation.model.ts
   252→│   ├── dto/
   253→│   └── types/
   254→├── cache/
   255→│   ├── cache.module.ts              # ioredis read/write + redlock
   256→│   └── cache.service.ts
   257→└── db/
   258→    └── db.module.ts                 # Knex pg read/write connections
   259→```
   260→
   261→## Security
   262→
   263→### OAuth
   264→
   265→- CSRF protection via `state` parameter (Arctic handles this)
   266→- PKCE for Google (Arctic native support)
   267→- OAuth access tokens encrypted at rest (AES-256) in `oauth_accounts.access_token`
   268→
   269→### JWT
   270→
   271→- Signed with HS256 (symmetric, single backend)
   272→- Access token payload: `{ user_id, session_id, role, company_id }` — minimal claims
   273→- Refresh token: opaque UUID stored alongside session in Redis (not a JWT)
   274→
   275→### Transport
   276→
   277→- Refresh token: `httpOnly`, `secure`, `sameSite: strict` cookie
   278→- Access token: response body, stored in memory on frontend (not localStorage)
   279→- CORS: restricted to frontend domain
   280→
   281→### Rate Limiting
   282→
   283→- `/auth/*` endpoints: 10 req/min per IP (`@nestjs/throttler`)
   284→- Token refresh: 5 req/min per user
   285→
   286→### Invitation Tokens
   287→
   288→- Cryptographically random (48 bytes, base64url)
   289→- 72hr expiry
   290→- Single use — `accepted_at` set on use
   291→
   292→## Infrastructure Dependencies
   293→
   294→### PostgreSQL
   295→
   296→- Write primary + read replica(s)
   297→- Knex.js via `nest-knexjs` with named connections (`KNEX_READ_CONNECTION`, `KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION`)
   298→- Client: `pg`
   299→
   300→### Redis
   301→
   302→- Write primary + read replica
   303→- ioredis with read/write separation (replicating recommendations-service pattern)
   304→- Keyv for cache abstraction
   305→- Redlock for distributed locking
   306→- Session TTL: 30 days
   307→
   308→### NPM Packages (New)
   309→
   310→- `arctic` — OAuth 2.0 provider helpers
   311→- `@nestjs/jwt` — JWT signing/verification
   312→- `pg` — PostgreSQL client
   313→- `knex` + `nest-knexjs` — query builder + NestJS integration
   314→- `ioredis` — Redis client
   315→- `@keyv/redis` — Keyv Redis adapter
   316→- `redlock` — distributed locking
   317→- `@nestjs/throttler` — rate limiting
   318→
   319→## Patterns (Following recommendations-service)
   320→
   321→### Database Layer
   322→
   323→- Knex read/write connection separation
   324→- Soft deletes (`.whereNull('deleted_at')` on all reads)
   325→- UUID primary keys via `randomUUID()` from `node:crypto`
   326→- Timestamps via `knex.fn.now()`
   327→- Input/output DTO validation via `plainToInstance()` + `validate()`
   328→- Thin service layer delegating to models
   329→- Table name constants (never raw strings)
   330→- Migrations wrapped in transactions, idempotent
   331→
   332→### Redis/Cache Layer
   333→
   334→- ioredis read/write clients with health-based fallback
   335→- Keyv cache managers with namespace isolation
   336→- Redlock for distributed locking
   337→- Recent-writes tracking for read-after-write consistency
   338→
```

  "content": "# Authentication & Authorization Design\n\n## Overview\n\nOAuth-based authentication (GitHub + Google) for a multi-tenant B2B application. Companies are the primary tenant. Admins create and manage companies; members (engineers) are invited and have read-only access to their own data and company statistics.\n\n## Stack\n\n- **OAuth**: Arctic (lightweight OAuth 2.0 library) with PKCE for both providers\n- **JWT**: `@nestjs/jwt` — access token (1hr) + refresh token (30d, opaque UUID)\n- **Sessions**: Redis (ioredis with read/write separation, replicating recommendations-service cache pattern)\n- **Database**: PostgreSQL with read/write replicas via Knex.js (`nest-knexjs`)\n- **Locking**: Redlock via Redis write client\n\n## Data Model\n\n### users\n\n| Column       | Type         | Constraints             |\n|--------------|--------------|-------------------------|\n| _id          | UUID         | PK                      |\n| email        | VARCHAR(255) | UNIQUE, NOT NULL        |\n| name         | VARCHAR(255) |                         |\n| avatar_url   | VARCHAR(512) |                         |\n| last_login_at| TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |\n| created_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |\n| updated_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |\n| deleted_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |\n\n- No `role` or `company_id` on this table. Role is derived from `company_members` based on the active company context.\n\n### oauth_accounts\n\n| Column       | Type                  | Constraints                     |\n|--------------|-----------------------|---------------------------------|\n| _id          | UUID                  | PK                              |\n| user_id      | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL        |\n| provider     | ENUM(github,google)   | NOT NULL                        |\n| provider_id  | VARCHAR(255)          | NOT NULL                        |\n| email        | VARCHAR(255)          |                                 |\n| access_token | TEXT                  | Encrypted (AES-256)             |\n| created_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |\n| updated_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |\n| deleted_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                            |\n\n- UNIQUE(provider, provider_id)\n- Supports multiple providers per user (account linking)\n- `email` captures the provider-reported email at link time. `users.email` is the canonical app email. Provider email changes are not auto-synced.\n\n### companies\n\n| Column     | Type         | Constraints             |\n|------------|--------------|-------------------------|\n| _id        | UUID         | PK                      |\n| name       | VARCHAR(255) | NOT NULL                |\n| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |\n| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |\n| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |\n\n### company_members\n\n| Column     | Type                  | Constraints                  |\n|------------|-----------------------|------------------------------|\n| _id        | UUID                  | PK                           |\n| user_id    | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL     |\n| company_id | UUID                  | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL |\n| role       | ENUM(admin,member)    | NOT NULL                     |\n| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |\n| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |\n| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                         |\n\n- UNIQUE(user_id, company_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL\n- Partial unique index: `UNIQUE(company_id) WHERE role = 'admin' AND deleted_at IS NULL` — enforces at most one admin per company at the DB level\n- Members: can belong to multiple companies\n\n### invitations\n\n| Column      | Type                | Constraints                   |\n|-------------|---------------------|-------------------------------|\n| _id         | UUID                | PK                            |\n| company_id  | UUID                | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL  |\n| invited_by  | UUID                | FK → users._id, NOT NULL      |\n| email       | VARCHAR(255)        | NOT NULL                      |\n| role        | ENUM(member)        | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'member'    |\n| token       | VARCHAR(255)        | UNIQUE, NOT NULL              |\n| expires_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL                      |\n| accepted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                          |\n| created_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()       |\n| updated_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()       |\n| deleted_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                          |\n\n- UNIQUE(company_id, email) WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL — prevents duplicate pending invitations\n- `invited_by` tracks which admin created the invitation (audit trail)\n- `role` restricted to `member` only — admins create companies via signup, not invitations\n\n## Auth Flow\n\n### OAuth Login Sequence\n\n1. Frontend redirects to `GET /auth/{provider}`\n2. Backend generates `state` param + PKCE `code_verifier`, stores both in a short-lived Redis key (keyed by state), redirects to provider\n3. Provider redirects back to `GET /auth/{provider}/callback` with authorization code\n4. Backend exchanges code for access token via Arctic (with PKCE `code_verifier`)\n5. Backend fetches user profile from provider\n6. Account resolution:\n   - **Existing oauth_account, same user** → issue auth code\n   - **Existing oauth_account, different user** → store linking token in Redis, redirect to frontend with linking prompt\n   - **No oauth_account, email matches existing user** → store linking token in Redis, redirect to frontend with linking prompt\n   - **No oauth_account, no email match** → create user, issue auth code\n7. Backend stores a short-lived authorization code in Redis (60s TTL), redirects to frontend with `?code=xxx`\n8. Frontend calls `POST /auth/token` with the code\n9. Backend validates code, creates session in Redis, returns access token in body + sets refresh token as httpOnly cookie\n\n### Account Linking Flow\n\nWhen the backend detects an email conflict during OAuth callback:\n\n1. Backend stores a temporary linking token in Redis (5min TTL) containing: `{ provider, provider_id, provider_email, existing_user_id }`\n2. Redirects to frontend: `/auth/link?linking_token=xxx`\n3. Frontend shows prompt: \"An account with this email already exists. Log in with your existing provider to link.\"\n4. User authenticates with their existing provider\n5. Frontend calls `POST /auth/link` with `{ linking_token }` + current session JWT\n6. Backend validates the linking token, verifies the authenticated user matches `existing_user_id`, creates the `oauth_accounts` entry\n\n### Token Exchange\n\nThe OAuth callback never returns JWTs directly. Instead:\n\n1. Callback stores a short-lived authorization code in Redis (60s TTL, single-use)\n2. Redirects to frontend with the code as a query parameter\n3. Frontend POSTs the code to `POST /auth/token`\n4. Backend validates code (exists in Redis, not expired, not used), deletes it\n5. Creates session in Redis\n6. Returns access token in response body\n7. Sets refresh token as httpOnly secure cookie\n\n### Refresh Token Storage\n\nRefresh tokens are stored as separate Redis keys:\n\n```\nKey:    refresh:{refresh_token_uuid}\nTTL:    30 days\nValue:  {\n  user_id:     \"uuid\",\n  session_id:  \"uuid\"\n}\n```\n\nOn rotation:\n1. Validate incoming refresh token exists in Redis\n2. Delete old refresh token key (atomically via Redis transaction)\n3. Generate new refresh token UUID\n4. Store new key with same session reference\n5. If a previously-rotated (deleted) refresh token is reused, it indicates potential theft — invalidate the entire session\n\n### Token Refresh\n\n1. Client sends refresh token (from httpOnly cookie)\n2. Backend looks up `refresh:{token}` in Redis, resolves to session\n3. Checks session still exists\n4. Issues new access token (1hr), rotates refresh token (30d)\n5. Updates session `last_active`\n\n### Request Authentication\n\nOn every authenticated request:\n1. Validate JWT signature + expiry\n2. Extract `session_id` from JWT claims\n3. Check session exists in Redis (key: `session:{user_id}:{session_id}`)\n4. If session gone → 401, force re-login\n5. Role and company_id come from session, not from a static user column\n\n### Logout\n\n- Delete session key from Redis → JWT becomes invalid immediately\n- Delete associated refresh token key\n\n## Session Structure (Redis)\n\n```\nKey:    session:{user_id}:{session_id}\nTTL:    30 days\n\nValue:  {\n  user_id:      \"uuid\",\n  session_id:   \"uuid\",\n  role:         \"admin\" | \"member\",\n  company_id:   \"uuid\" | null,\n  provider:     \"github\" | \"google\",\n  ip:           \"x.x.x.x\",\n  user_agent:   \"...\",\n  created_at:   \"ISO timestamp\",\n  last_active:  \"ISO timestamp\"\n}\n```\n\nAdditionally, maintain a set for O(1) session lookup per user:\n```\nKey:    sessions:{user_id}\nValue:  SET of session_ids\n```\n\n### Session Operations\n\n- **Create** — on login, write session to Redis with 30d TTL, add session_id to `sessions:{user_id}` set\n- **Validate** — on every request, auth guard checks session key exists\n- **Refresh** — on token refresh, update `last_active`\n- **Destroy** — on logout, delete session key + remove from set + delete refresh token\n- **Destroy all** — read `sessions:{user_id}` set, delete all session keys + all refresh tokens, delete the set\n\n### Session Invalidation on Membership Changes\n\nWhen a user is removed from a company or a company is deleted:\n- Find all sessions where `company_id` matches the affected company\n- Destroy those sessions (forces re-login, user will get a new session with a different active company or no company)\n\n### Company Context Switching (Members)\n\n- Member calls `POST /users/me/company` with `company_id`\n- Backend verifies user has a `company_members` entry for that company\n- Updates `role` and `company_id` in the Redis session (role comes from `company_members.role`)\n- Next access token issued includes the new company context\n\n### Invitation Acceptance Flow\n\n1. Recipient clicks link: `GET /invitations/accept?token=xxx`\n2. Backend validates token (exists, not expired, not accepted)\n3. If user is already authenticated:\n   - Check if authenticated user's email matches invitation email\n   - If match: create `company_members` entry, mark invitation as accepted\n   - If no match: reject with error\n4. If user is not authenticated:\n   - Redirect to OAuth with invitation token stored in session state\n   - After OAuth completes, check if the authenticated email matches invitation email\n   - If match: create `company_members` entry, mark invitation as accepted\n   - If no match: reject with error (\"You must sign in with the email the invitation was sent to\")\n\n## API Endpoints\n\n### OAuth\n\n| Method | Path                       | Description                        | Auth     |\n|--------|----------------------------|------------------------------------|----------|\n| GET    | /auth/github               | Redirect to GitHub OAuth           | Public   |\n| GET    | /auth/github/callback      | Handle GitHub callback             | Public   |\n| GET    | /auth/google               | Redirect to Google OAuth           | Public   |\n| GET    | /auth/google/callback      | Handle Google callback             | Public   |\n| POST   | /auth/token                | Exchange auth code for JWT         | Public   |\n| POST   | /auth/refresh              | Refresh access token               | Cookie   |\n| POST   | /auth/logout               | Destroy current session            | Auth     |\n| POST   | /auth/logout/all           | Destroy all sessions for user      | Auth     |\n| POST   | /auth/link                 | Link OAuth to existing user        | Auth     |\n\n### Signup\n\n| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |\n|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|\n| POST   | /auth/signup/company       | Create company after OAuth     | Auth     |\n\n### User\n\n| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |\n|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|\n| GET    | /users/me                  | Current user + active company  | Auth     |\n| POST   | /users/me/company          | Switch active company context  | Auth     |\n\n### Company\n\n| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth       |\n|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|------------|\n| GET    | /companies/:id             | Company details                | Member+    |\n\n- User must be a member of the company (checked via `company_members`)\n\n### Invitations (Admin only)\n\n| Method | Path                              | Description              | Auth     |\n|--------|-----------------------------------|--------------------------|----------|\n| POST   | /companies/:id/invitations        | Invite engineer by email | Admin    |\n| GET    | /companies/:id/invitations        | List pending invitations | Admin    |\n| DELETE | /companies/:id/invitations/:invId | Revoke invitation        | Admin    |\n| GET    | /invitations/accept?token=xxx     | Accept invite → OAuth    | Public   |\n\n## Module Architecture\n\n```\nbackend/src/modules/\n├── auth/\n│   ├── auth.module.ts\n│   ├── auth.controller.ts\n│   ├── auth.service.ts\n│   ├── strategies/\n│   │   ├── github.strategy.ts      # Arctic GitHub provider\n│   │   └── google.strategy.ts      # Arctic Google provider\n│   ├── guards/\n│   │   ├── auth.guard.ts           # JWT + session validation\n│   │   └── roles.guard.ts          # Role-based access\n│   ├── decorators/\n│   │   ├── current-user.decorator.ts\n│   │   ├── roles.decorator.ts\n│   │   └── public.decorator.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   ├── types/\n│   └── constants/\n├── session/\n│   ├── session.module.ts\n│   ├── session.service.ts\n│   └── types/\n├── user/\n│   ├── user.module.ts\n│   ├── user.service.ts\n│   ├── user.model.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   └── types/\n├── oauth-account/\n│   ├── oauth-account.module.ts\n│   ├── oauth-account.service.ts\n│   ├── oauth-account.model.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   └── types/\n├── company/\n│   ├── company.module.ts\n│   ├── company.service.ts\n│   ├── company.model.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   └── types/\n├── company-member/\n│   ├── company-member.module.ts\n│   ├── company-member.service.ts\n│   ├── company-member.model.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   └── types/\n├── invitation/\n│   ├── invitation.module.ts\n│   ├── invitation.service.ts\n│   ├── invitation.model.ts\n│   ├── dto/\n│   └── types/\n├── cache/\n│   ├── cache.module.ts              # ioredis read/write + redlock\n│   └── cache.service.ts\n└── db/\n    └── db.module.ts                 # Knex pg read/write connections\n```\n\n## Security\n\n### OAuth\n\n- CSRF protection via `state` parameter (Arctic handles this)\n- PKCE for both GitHub and Google (Arctic native support)\n- OAuth access tokens encrypted at rest (AES-256) in `oauth_accounts.access_token`\n\n### JWT\n\n- Signed with HS256 (symmetric, single backend)\n- Access token payload: `{ user_id, session_id, role, company_id }` — minimal claims\n- Refresh token: opaque UUID stored as separate Redis key (not a JWT)\n- Future consideration: if multi-service token verification is needed (e.g., separate websocket service), migrate to RS256/ES256 with public key distribution\n\n### Transport\n\n- Refresh token: `httpOnly`, `secure`, `sameSite: strict` cookie\n- Access token: response body, stored in memory on frontend (not localStorage)\n- CORS: restricted to frontend domain\n\n### Rate Limiting\n\n- `/auth/*` endpoints: 10 req/min per IP (`@nestjs/throttler`)\n- Token refresh: 5 req/min per user\n- Invitation creation: 20 req/hr per company\n\n### Invitation Tokens\n\n- Cryptographically random (48 bytes, base64url)\n- 72hr expiry\n- Single use — `accepted_at` set on use\n\n### Refresh Token Reuse Detection\n\nWhen a previously-rotated refresh token is reused, it indicates potential token theft. Response:\n1. Invalidate the entire session (delete session + all refresh tokens for that session)\n2. Log a security event\n3. Return 401\n\n## Infrastructure Dependencies\n\n### PostgreSQL\n\n- Write primary + read replica(s)\n- Knex.js via `nest-knexjs` with named connections (`KNEX_READ_CONNECTION`, `KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION`)\n- Client: `pg`\n\n### Redis\n\n- Write primary + read replica\n- ioredis with read/write separation (replicating recommendations-service pattern)\n- Keyv for cache abstraction\n- Redlock for distributed locking\n- Session TTL: 30 days\n\n### NPM Packages (New)\n\n- `arctic` — OAuth 2.0 provider helpers\n- `@nestjs/jwt` — JWT signing/verification\n- `pg` — PostgreSQL client\n- `knex` + `nest-knexjs` — query builder + NestJS integration\n- `ioredis` — Redis client\n- `@keyv/redis` — Keyv Redis adapter\n- `redlock` — distributed locking\n- `@nestjs/throttler` — rate limiting\n\n## Patterns (Following recommendations-service)\n\n### Database Layer\n\n- Knex read/write connection separation\n- Soft deletes (`.whereNull('deleted_at')` on all reads)\n- UUID primary keys via `randomUUID()` from `node:crypto`\n- Timestamps via `knex.fn.now()`\n- Input/output DTO validation via `plainToInstance()` + `validate()`\n- Thin service layer delegating to models\n- Table name constants (never raw strings)\n- Migrations wrapped in transactions, idempotent\n\n### Redis/Cache Layer\n\n- ioredis read/write clients with health-based fallback\n- Keyv cache managers with namespace isolation\n- Redlock for distributed locking\n- Recent-writes tracking for read-after-write consistency\n\n### Query Helpers\n\nConsider creating a Knex query helper or database view that joins `users` with `company_members` for the active company context. This join will be used frequently across the application.\n"
}
```

```
     1→# Authentication & Authorization Design
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→OAuth-based authentication (GitHub + Google) for a multi-tenant B2B application. Companies are the primary tenant. Admins create and manage companies; members (engineers) are invited and have read-only access to their own data and company statistics.
     6→
     7→## Stack
     8→
     9→- **OAuth**: Arctic (lightweight OAuth 2.0 library) with PKCE for both providers
    10→- **JWT**: `@nestjs/jwt` — access token (1hr) + refresh token (30d, opaque UUID)
    11→- **Sessions**: Redis (ioredis with read/write separation, replicating recommendations-service cache pattern)
    12→- **Database**: PostgreSQL with read/write replicas via Knex.js (`nest-knexjs`)
    13→- **Locking**: Redlock via Redis write client
    14→
    15→## Data Model
    16→
    17→### users
    18→
    19→| Column       | Type         | Constraints             |
    20→|--------------|--------------|-------------------------|
    21→| _id          | UUID         | PK                      |
    22→| email        | VARCHAR(255) | UNIQUE, NOT NULL        |
    23→| name         | VARCHAR(255) |                         |
    24→| avatar_url   | VARCHAR(512) |                         |
    25→| last_login_at| TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |
    26→| created_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    27→| updated_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    28→| deleted_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |
    29→
    30→- No `role` or `company_id` on this table. Role is derived from `company_members` based on the active company context.
    31→
    32→### oauth_accounts
    33→
    34→| Column       | Type                  | Constraints                     |
    35→|--------------|-----------------------|---------------------------------|
    36→| _id          | UUID                  | PK                              |
    37→| user_id      | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL        |
    38→| provider     | ENUM(github,google)   | NOT NULL                        |
    39→| provider_id  | VARCHAR(255)          | NOT NULL                        |
    40→| email        | VARCHAR(255)          |                                 |
    41→| access_token | TEXT                  | Encrypted (AES-256)             |
    42→| created_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |
    43→| updated_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()         |
    44→| deleted_at   | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                            |
    45→
    46→- UNIQUE(provider, provider_id)
    47→- Supports multiple providers per user (account linking)
    48→- `email` captures the provider-reported email at link time. `users.email` is the canonical app email. Provider email changes are not auto-synced.
    49→
    50→### companies
    51→
    52→| Column     | Type         | Constraints             |
    53→|------------|--------------|-------------------------|
    54→| _id        | UUID         | PK                      |
    55→| name       | VARCHAR(255) | NOT NULL                |
    56→| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    57→| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now() |
    58→| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ  | NULL                    |
    59→
    60→### company_members
    61→
    62→| Column     | Type                  | Constraints                  |
    63→|------------|-----------------------|------------------------------|
    64→| _id        | UUID                  | PK                           |
    65→| user_id    | UUID                  | FK → users._id, NOT NULL     |
    66→| company_id | UUID                  | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL |
    67→| role       | ENUM(admin,member)    | NOT NULL                     |
    68→| created_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    69→| updated_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()      |
    70→| deleted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ           | NULL                         |
    71→
    72→- UNIQUE(user_id, company_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL
    73→- Partial unique index: `UNIQUE(company_id) WHERE role = 'admin' AND deleted_at IS NULL` — enforces at most one admin per company at the DB level
    74→- Members: can belong to multiple companies
    75→
    76→### invitations
    77→
    78→| Column      | Type                | Constraints                   |
    79→|-------------|---------------------|-------------------------------|
    80→| _id         | UUID                | PK                            |
    81→| company_id  | UUID                | FK → companies._id, NOT NULL  |
    82→| invited_by  | UUID                | FK → users._id, NOT NULL      |
    83→| email       | VARCHAR(255)        | NOT NULL                      |
    84→| role        | ENUM(member)        | NOT NULL, DEFAULT 'member'    |
    85→| token       | VARCHAR(255)        | UNIQUE, NOT NULL              |
    86→| expires_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL                      |
    87→| accepted_at | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                          |
    88→| created_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()       |
    89→| updated_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NOT NULL, DEFAULT now()       |
    90→| deleted_at  | TIMESTAMPTZ         | NULL                          |
    91→
    92→- UNIQUE(company_id, email) WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL — prevents duplicate pending invitations
    93→- `invited_by` tracks which admin created the invitation (audit trail)
    94→- `role` restricted to `member` only — admins create companies via signup, not invitations
    95→
    96→## Auth Flow
    97→
    98→### OAuth Login Sequence
    99→
   100→1. Frontend redirects to `GET /auth/{provider}`
   101→2. Backend generates `state` param + PKCE `code_verifier`, stores both in a short-lived Redis key (keyed by state), redirects to provider
   102→3. Provider redirects back to `GET /auth/{provider}/callback` with authorization code
   103→4. Backend exchanges code for access token via Arctic (with PKCE `code_verifier`)
   104→5. Backend fetches user profile from provider
   105→6. Account resolution:
   106→   - **Existing oauth_account, same user** → issue auth code
   107→   - **Existing oauth_account, different user** → store linking token in Redis, redirect to frontend with linking prompt
   108→   - **No oauth_account, email matches existing user** → store linking token in Redis, redirect to frontend with linking prompt
   109→   - **No oauth_account, no email match** → create user, issue auth code
   110→7. Backend stores a short-lived authorization code in Redis (60s TTL), redirects to frontend with `?code=xxx`
   111→8. Frontend calls `POST /auth/token` with the code
   112→9. Backend validates code, creates session in Redis, returns access token in body + sets refresh token as httpOnly cookie
   113→
   114→### Account Linking Flow
   115→
   116→When the backend detects an email conflict during OAuth callback:
   117→
   118→1. Backend stores a temporary linking token in Redis (5min TTL) containing: `{ provider, provider_id, provider_email, existing_user_id }`
   119→2. Redirects to frontend: `/auth/link?linking_token=xxx`
   120→3. Frontend shows prompt: "An account with this email already exists. Log in with your existing provider to link."
   121→4. User authenticates with their existing provider
   122→5. Frontend calls `POST /auth/link` with `{ linking_token }` + current session JWT
   123→6. Backend validates the linking token, verifies the authenticated user matches `existing_user_id`, creates the `oauth_accounts` entry
   124→
   125→### Token Exchange
   126→
   127→The OAuth callback never returns JWTs directly. Instead:
   128→
   129→1. Callback stores a short-lived authorization code in Redis (60s TTL, single-use)
   130→2. Redirects to frontend with the code as a query parameter
   131→3. Frontend POSTs the code to `POST /auth/token`
   132→4. Backend validates code (exists in Redis, not expired, not used), deletes it
   133→5. Creates session in Redis
   134→6. Returns access token in response body
   135→7. Sets refresh token as httpOnly secure cookie
   136→
   137→### Refresh Token Storage
   138→
   139→Refresh tokens are stored as separate Redis keys:
   140→
   141→```
   142→Key:    refresh:{refresh_token_uuid}
   143→TTL:    30 days
   144→Value:  {
   145→  user_id:     "uuid",
   146→  session_id:  "uuid"
   147→}
   148→```
   149→
   150→On rotation:
   151→1. Validate incoming refresh token exists in Redis
   152→2. Delete old refresh token key (atomically via Redis transaction)
   153→3. Generate new refresh token UUID
   154→4. Store new key with same session reference
   155→5. If a previously-rotated (deleted) refresh token is reused, it indicates potential theft — invalidate the entire session
   156→
   157→### Token Refresh
   158→
   159→1. Client sends refresh token (from httpOnly cookie)
   160→2. Backend looks up `refresh:{token}` in Redis, resolves to session
   161→3. Checks session still exists
   162→4. Issues new access token (1hr), rotates refresh token (30d)
   163→5. Updates session `last_active`
   164→
   165→### Request Authentication
   166→
   167→On every authenticated request:
   168→1. Validate JWT signature + expiry
   169→2. Extract `session_id` from JWT claims
   170→3. Check session exists in Redis (key: `session:{user_id}:{session_id}`)
   171→4. If session gone → 401, force re-login
   172→5. Role and company_id come from session, not from a static user column
   173→
   174→### Logout
   175→
   176→- Delete session key from Redis → JWT becomes invalid immediately
   177→- Delete associated refresh token key
   178→
   179→## Session Structure (Redis)
   180→
   181→```
   182→Key:    session:{user_id}:{session_id}
   183→TTL:    30 days
   184→
   185→Value:  {
   186→  user_id:      "uuid",
   187→  session_id:   "uuid",
   188→  role:         "admin" | "member",
   189→  company_id:   "uuid" | null,
   190→  provider:     "github" | "google",
   191→  ip:           "x.x.x.x",
   192→  user_agent:   "...",
   193→  created_at:   "ISO timestamp",
   194→  last_active:  "ISO timestamp"
   195→}
   196→```
   197→
   198→Additionally, maintain a set for O(1) session lookup per user:
   199→```
   200→Key:    sessions:{user_id}
   201→Value:  SET of session_ids
   202→```
   203→
   204→### Session Operations
   205→
   206→- **Create** — on login, write session to Redis with 30d TTL, add session_id to `sessions:{user_id}` set
   207→- **Validate** — on every request, auth guard checks session key exists
   208→- **Refresh** — on token refresh, update `last_active`
   209→- **Destroy** — on logout, delete session key + remove from set + delete refresh token
   210→- **Destroy all** — read `sessions:{user_id}` set, delete all session keys + all refresh tokens, delete the set
   211→
   212→### Session Invalidation on Membership Changes
   213→
   214→When a user is removed from a company or a company is deleted:
   215→- Find all sessions where `company_id` matches the affected company
   216→- Destroy those sessions (forces re-login, user will get a new session with a different active company or no company)
   217→
   218→### Company Context Switching (Members)
   219→
   220→- Member calls `POST /users/me/company` with `company_id`
   221→- Backend verifies user has a `company_members` entry for that company
   222→- Updates `role` and `company_id` in the Redis session (role comes from `company_members.role`)
   223→- Next access token issued includes the new company context
   224→
   225→### Invitation Acceptance Flow
   226→
   227→1. Recipient clicks link: `GET /invitations/accept?token=xxx`
   228→2. Backend validates token (exists, not expired, not accepted)
   229→3. If user is already authenticated:
   230→   - Check if authenticated user's email matches invitation email
   231→   - If match: create `company_members` entry, mark invitation as accepted
   232→   - If no match: reject with error
   233→4. If user is not authenticated:
   234→   - Redirect to OAuth with invitation token stored in session state
   235→   - After OAuth completes, check if the authenticated email matches invitation email
   236→   - If match: create `company_members` entry, mark invitation as accepted
   237→   - If no match: reject with error ("You must sign in with the email the invitation was sent to")
   238→
   239→## API Endpoints
   240→
   241→### OAuth
   242→
   243→| Method | Path                       | Description                        | Auth     |
   244→|--------|----------------------------|------------------------------------|----------|
   245→| GET    | /auth/github               | Redirect to GitHub OAuth           | Public   |
   246→| GET    | /auth/github/callback      | Handle GitHub callback             | Public   |
   247→| GET    | /auth/google               | Redirect to Google OAuth           | Public   |
   248→| GET    | /auth/google/callback      | Handle Google callback             | Public   |
   249→| POST   | /auth/token                | Exchange auth code for JWT         | Public   |
   250→| POST   | /auth/refresh              | Refresh access token               | Cookie   |
   251→| POST   | /auth/logout               | Destroy current session            | Auth     |
   252→| POST   | /auth/logout/all           | Destroy all sessions for user      | Auth     |
   253→| POST   | /auth/link                 | Link OAuth to existing user        | Auth     |
   254→
   255→### Signup
   256→
   257→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   258→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   259→| POST   | /auth/signup/company       | Create company after OAuth     | Auth     |
   260→
   261→### User
   262→
   263→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth     |
   264→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|----------|
   265→| GET    | /users/me                  | Current user + active company  | Auth     |
   266→| POST   | /users/me/company          | Switch active company context  | Auth     |
   267→
   268→### Company
   269→
   270→| Method | Path                       | Description                    | Auth       |
   271→|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|------------|
   272→| GET    | /companies/:id             | Company details                | Member+    |
   273→
   274→- User must be a member of the company (checked via `company_members`)
   275→
   276→### Invitations (Admin only)
   277→
   278→| Method | Path                              | Description              | Auth     |
   279→|--------|-----------------------------------|--------------------------|----------|
   280→| POST   | /companies/:id/invitations        | Invite engineer by email | Admin    |
   281→| GET    | /companies/:id/invitations        | List pending invitations | Admin    |
   282→| DELETE | /companies/:id/invitations/:invId | Revoke invitation        | Admin    |
   283→| GET    | /invitations/accept?token=xxx     | Accept invite → OAuth    | Public   |
   284→
   285→## Module Architecture
   286→
   287→```
   288→backend/src/modules/
   289→├── auth/
   290→│   ├── auth.module.ts
   291→│   ├── auth.controller.ts
   292→│   ├── auth.service.ts
   293→│   ├── strategies/

   345→```
   346→
   347→## Security
   348→
   349→### OAuth
   350→
   351→- CSRF protection via `state` parameter (Arctic handles this)
   352→- PKCE for both GitHub and Google (Arctic native support)
   353→- OAuth access tokens encrypted at rest (AES-256) in `oauth_accounts.access_token`
   354→
   355→### JWT
   356→
   357→- Signed with HS256 (symmetric, single backend)
   358→- Access token payload: `{ user_id, session_id, role, company_id }` — minimal claims
   359→- Refresh token: opaque UUID stored as separate Redis key (not a JWT)
   360→- Future consideration: if multi-service token verification is needed (e.g., separate websocket service), migrate to RS256/ES256 with public key distribution
   361→
   362→### Transport
   363→
   364→- Refresh token: `httpOnly`, `secure`, `sameSite: strict` cookie
   365→- Access token: response body, stored in memory on frontend (not localStorage)
   366→- CORS: restricted to frontend domain
   367→
   368→### Rate Limiting
   369→
   370→- `/auth/*` endpoints: 10 req/min per IP (`@nestjs/throttler`)
   371→- Token refresh: 5 req/min per user
   372→- Invitation creation: 20 req/hr per company
   373→
   374→### Invitation Tokens
   375→
   376→- Cryptographically random (48 bytes, base64url)
   377→- 72hr expiry
   378→- Single use — `accepted_at` set on use
   379→
   380→### Refresh Token Reuse Detection
   381→
   382→When a previously-rotated refresh token is reused, it indicates potential token theft. Response:
   383→1. Invalidate the entire session (delete session + all refresh tokens for that session)
   384→2. Log a security event
   385→3. Return 401
   386→
   387→## Infrastructure Dependencies
   388→
   389→### PostgreSQL
   390→
   391→- Write primary + read replica(s)
   392→- Knex.js via `nest-knexjs` with named connections (`KNEX_READ_CONNECTION`, `KNEX_WRITE_CONNECTION`)
   393→- Client: `pg`
   394→
   395→### Redis
   396→
   397→- Write primary + read replica
   398→- ioredis with read/write separation (replicating recommendations-service pattern)
   399→- Keyv for cache abstraction
   400→- Redlock for distributed locking
   401→- Session TTL: 30 days
   402→
   403→### NPM Packages (New)
   404→
   405→- `arctic` — OAuth 2.0 provider helpers
   406→- `@nestjs/jwt` — JWT signing/verification
   407→- `pg` — PostgreSQL client
   408→- `knex` + `nest-knexjs` — query builder + NestJS integration
   409→- `ioredis` — Redis client
   410→- `@keyv/redis` — Keyv Redis adapter
   411→- `redlock` — distributed locking
   412→- `@nestjs/throttler` — rate limiting
   413→
   414→## Patterns (Following recommendations-service)
   415→
   416→### Database Layer
   417→
   418→- Knex read/write connection separation
   419→- Soft deletes (`.whereNull('deleted_at')` on all reads)
   420→- UUID primary keys via `randomUUID()` from `node:crypto`
   421→- Timestamps via `knex.fn.now()`
   422→- Input/output DTO validation via `plainToInstance()` + `validate()`
   423→- Thin service layer delegating to models
   424→- Table name constants (never raw strings)
   425→- Migrations wrapped in transactions, idempotent
   426→
   427→### Redis/Cache Layer
   428→
   429→- ioredis read/write clients with health-based fallback
   430→- Keyv cache managers with namespace isolation
   431→- Redlock for distributed locking
   432→- Recent-writes tracking for read-after-write consistency
   433→
   434→### Query Helpers
   435→
   436→Consider creating a Knex query helper or database view that joins `users` with `company_members` for the active company context. This join will be used frequently across the application.
   437→
```

### 3. main.ts - Fastify Configuration & Bootstrap

**Location:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/main.ts`

**Key Configuration:**

- **Adapter:** `FastifyAdapter` with `trustProxy: true`
- **Logging:** Dev mode logs all levels (log, error, warn, fatal, debug, verbose); production logs only log, error, warn, fatal
- **CORS:** Enabled globally with `origin: true, credentials: true`
- **Global Validation Pipe:** Applied with `transform: true, whitelist: true, forbidNonWhitelisted: true`
- **Global Prefix:** Set via `PORT` env variable (default 9069), excluded from liveness & readiness probes
- **Swagger:** Setup via `setupSwagger()` utility
- **Graceful Shutdown:** Only enabled in non-dev environments
- **Server Binding:** Listens on `0.0.0.0` (all interfaces)
- **Hot Module Replacement:** Supports webpack HMR for dev mode

---

### 4. env-config Module - Environment Variable Validation & Access

**Location:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/env-config/`

**Structure:**

**env-config.module.ts:**
- Imports `ConfigModule.forRoot()` with custom `loadEnvVariables()` loader
- Sets `validatePredefined: false` and `validationOptions: { allowUnknown: true, abortEarly: false }`
- Exports `EnvConfigService` for dependency injection

**env-config.service.ts:**
- Wraps NestJS `ConfigService`
- `get<T>({ key, converter? })`: Retrieves env var with optional type conversion
- Throws error if env variable is undefined
- Logs all fetches

**env-validations.config.ts:**
- Uses `class-validator` decorators (plainToInstance + validateSync)
- Defines `EnvironmentVariables` class with validation rules
- `validate()` function validates entire config object at startup
- Returns validated instance on success, throws error on validation failure

**Environment Variables Currently Defined:**

| Variable | Type | Validators | Example |
|----------|------|-----------|---------|
| `NODE_ENV` | enum | `@IsEnum(Environment)` | `development`, `staging`, `production`, `test` |
| `PORT` | number | `@IsNumber()`, `@Min(0)`, `@Max(65535)` | `9069` |
| `GLOBAL_PREFIX` | string | `@IsString()`, `@IsNotEmpty()` | `api` |
| `SERVER_NAME` | string | `@IsString()`, `@IsNotEmpty()` | `webapp-backend` |
| `IS_COMPACT_LOGS` | boolean | `@IsBoolean()` | `false` |
| `IS_JSON_LIKE_LOGS` | boolean | `@IsBoolean()` | `true` |
| `GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_DELAY` | number | `@IsNumber()`, `@Min(0)`, `@Max(60000)` | `5000` |

**How Env Loading Works:**

1. **Development/Test:** Loads from `.env` or `.env.test` files using `dotenv.parse()`
2. **Staging/Production:** Validates `process.env` directly (expects vars already set)
3. Validation happens at **startup** via `validate()` function
4. **Two access patterns:**
   - Via `EnvConfigService` (DI): `configService.get<T>({ key: ENV_VAR })`
   - Via `getEnv()` utility (standalone): `getEnv<T>({ key: ENV_VAR })`

**Utility Functions:**

- `getEnvFile()`: Returns `.env` or `.env.test` based on NODE_ENV
- `getEnvVariables()`: Maps `process.env` to typed object
- `loadEnvVariables()`: Returns ConfigFactory array for ConfigModule
- `isDevEnv()`, `isProdEnv()`, `isTestEnv()`, `isStagingEnv()`: Environment detection helpers

### 5. health Module - Health Check Endpoints

**Location:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/health/`

**health.module.ts:**
- Imports `TerminusModule.forRoot()` with graceful shutdown timeout from `GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN_DELAY`
- Imports `HttpModule` for HTTP checks
- Provides `HealthController` and `HealthService`

**health.controller.ts:**
- **GET /liveness**: Simple endpoint returning `{ status: 'up' }` (no decorators)
- **GET /readiness**: Performs full health checks via Terminus:
  - Calls `HealthService.isAppHealthy()` for app health
  - Calls `MemoryHealthIndicator.checkHeap()` with 500MB threshold
  - Returns `shutting_down` status if SIGTERM received
  - Swagger-documented with `@ApiTags('Health')`, `@ApiOperation()`, `@ApiOkResponse()`

**health.service.ts:**
- `isAppHealthy()`: Returns `HealthIndicatorResult` with app status, uptime, and timestamp

   - ts-jest transformation
   - 20-second timeout
   - Node test environment

2. **jest-unit.config.ts** - Unit test runner
   - testRegex: `.*\.unit-spec\.ts$`

3. **jest-unit.coverage.config.ts** - Unit tests with 95% coverage enforcement

4. **jest-e2e.config.ts** - E2E test runner
   - testRegex: `.*\.e2e-spec\.ts$`
   - runInBand: true
   - forceExit: true

5. **jest-e2e.coverage.config.ts** - E2E with 95% coverage (controllers/services only)

6. **jest.coverage.config.ts** - Base coverage config with 95% thresholds

**Test Examples Found:**

- Unit test pattern: `src/unit-tests/health.service.unit-spec.ts` (mocking dependencies, Logger spy)
- E2E test pattern: `test/health.module.e2e-spec.ts` (real Terminus setup, supertest HTTP assertions)
- Env config test: `test/env-config.module.e2e-spec.ts`

---

### 13. Git & Node Version Management

- **.node-version:** `24.13.1`
- **.nvmrc:** `v24.13.1`
- **Husky pre-commit hook:** Runs `npm run code-quality-check` (lint + format + type-check)

---

### 14. Utility Functions Summary

**common.util.ts:**
- `isTestEnv()`: Returns true if NODE_ENV === 'test'
- `isProdEnv()`: Returns true if NODE_ENV === 'production'
- `isStagingEnv()`: Returns true if NODE_ENV === 'staging'
- `isDevEnv()`: Returns true if NODE_ENV === 'development'

**get-env-file.util.ts:**
- `getEnvFile()`: Returns `.env` or `.env.test`
- `getEnvVariables()`: Maps process.env to typed object
- `loadEnvVariables()`: Returns ConfigFactory array for ConfigModule
- Handles validation at startup

**get-env-variable.util.ts:**
- `getEnv<T>()`: Standalone utility to access env vars without DI

**setup-swagger.util.ts:**
- `setupSwagger()`: Configures Swagger/OpenAPI
- Sets title, description, version
- Adds Bearer auth support
- Mounts at `/{globalPrefix}/swagger`

---

## Key Architectural Patterns

1. **Modular Structure:** Feature-based modules (app, health, env-config) with clear concerns
2. **Env Validation:** Startup validation via class-validator, strict typing throughout
3. **Health Checks:** Liveness (simple) + Readiness (full chain) pattern
4. **DI Container:** NestJS modules with explicit imports/exports
5. **Hot Reload:** Webpack + webpack-hot-middleware for dev
6. **Strict TypeScript:** ES2023, all checks enabled, path aliases for clean imports
7. **Comprehensive Testing:** Unit + E2E with 95% coverage requirement
8. **Code Quality:** ESLint v9 flat config, Prettier, Husky pre-commit hooks
9. **Two Env Access Patterns:** Dependency injection (EnvConfigService) or standalone (getEnv)

---

This is the complete foundation. All source code is well-organized, strictly typed, comprehensively tested, and documented in CLAUDE.md. The backend is ready for feature development following the established patterns.

ALGORITHM = 'aes-256-gcm';\nconst IV_LENGTH = 16;\nconst AUTH_TAG_LENGTH = 16;\n\nexport function encrypt({ plaintext, key }: { plaintext: string; key: string }): string {\n  const iv = randomBytes(IV_LENGTH);\n  const cipher = createCipheriv(ALGORITHM, Buffer.from(key, 'hex'), iv);\n  const encrypted = Buffer.concat([cipher.update(plaintext, 'utf8'), cipher.final()]);\n  const authTag = cipher.getAuthTag();\n  return Buffer.concat([iv, authTag, encrypted]).toString('base64url');\n}\n\nexport function decrypt({ ciphertext, key }: { ciphertext: string; key: string }): string {\n  const data = Buffer.from(ciphertext, 'base64url');\n  const iv = data.subarray(0, IV_LENGTH);\n  const authTag = data.subarray(IV_LENGTH, IV_LENGTH + AUTH_TAG_LENGTH);\n  const encrypted = data.subarray(IV_LENGTH + AUTH_TAG_LENGTH);\n  const decipher = createDecipheriv(ALGORITHM, Buffer.from(key, 'hex'), iv);\n  decipher.setAuthTag(authTag);\n  return decipher.update(encrypted) + decipher.final('utf8');\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern encryption`\nExpected: PASS\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: failing tests for AuthGuard**\n\nTest scenarios:\n- Public routes (decorated with `@Public()`) → allow through\n- Missing Authorization header → 401\n- Invalid JWT → 401\n- Valid JWT but session not in Redis → 401\n- Valid JWT and session exists → attach user to request, allow through\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement AuthGuard**\n\n```typescript\n@Injectable()\nexport class AuthGuard implements CanActivate {\n  constructor(\n    private readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n    private readonly sessionService: SessionService,\n    private readonly reflector: Reflector,\n  ) {}\n\n  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n    // Check @Public() decorator\n    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n      context.getHandler(),\n      context.getClass(),\n    ]);\n    if (isPublic) return true;\n\n    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest<FastifyRequest>();\n    const token = this.extractTokenFromHeader({ request });\n    if (!token) throw new UnauthorizedException();\n\n    const payload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<IJwtPayload>(token);\n    const session = await this.sessionService.getSession({\n      userId: payload.user_id,\n      sessionId: payload.session_id,\n    });\n    if (!session) throw new UnauthorizedException();\n\n    (request as any).user = {\n      user_id: payload.user_id,\n      session_id: payload.session_id,\n      role: session.role,\n      company_id: session.company_id,\n    } satisfies IRequestUser;\n\n    return true;\n  }\n\n  private extractTokenFromHeader({ request }: { request: FastifyRequest }): string | null {\n    const [type, token] = request.headers.authorization?.split(' ') ?? [];\n    return type === 'Bearer' ? token ?? null : null;\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Run tests — verify pass**\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Write failing tests for RolesGuard**\n\nTest: missing roles metadata → allow. User role not in required roles → 403. User role matches → allow.\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Implement RolesGuard**\n\n```typescript\n@Injectable()\nexport class RolesGuard implements CanActivate {\n  constructor(private readonly reflector: Reflector) {}\n\n  canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {\n    const requiredRoles = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<string[]>(ROLES_KEY, [\n      context.getHandler(),\n      context.getClass(),\n    ]);\n    if (!requiredRoles) return true;\n\n    const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();\n    const user: IRequestUser = request.user;\n    if (!user) throw new ForbiddenException();\n\n    if (!requiredRoles.includes(user.role)) throw new ForbiddenException();\n    return true;\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 7: Run all guard tests**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern guard`\nExpected: PASS\n\n- [ ] **Step 8: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/src/modules/auth/guards/ backend/src/modules/auth/types/ backend/src/unit-tests/auth.guard.unit-spec.ts backend/src/unit-tests/roles.guard.unit-spec.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat: add auth guard (JWT + session validation) and roles guard\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 14: OAuth Strategies (Arctic)\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/auth/strategies/github.strategy.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/auth/strategies/google.strategy.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/auth/constants/auth.constant.ts`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create auth constants**\n\n```typescript\n// auth.constant.ts\nexport const AUTH_CODE_TTL = 60; codeVerifier: string }): URL {\n    return this.github.createAuthorizationURL(state, codeVerifier, ['user:email']);\n  }\n\n  async validateAuthorizationCode({ code, codeVerifier }: { code: string; codeVerifier: string }): Promise<{ accessToken: string }> {\n    const tokens = await this.github.validateAuthorizationCode(code, codeVerifier);\n    return { accessToken: tokens.accessToken() };\n  }\n\n  async getUserProfile({ accessToken }: { accessToken: string }): Promise<IOAuthProfile> {\n    const response = await fetch('https://api.github.com/user', {\n      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },\n    });\n    const data = await response.json();\n\n    // Fetch primary email if not public\n    let email = data.email;\n    if (!email) {\n      const emailResponse = await fetch('https://api.github.com/user/emails', {\n        headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${accessToken}` },\n      });\n      const emails = await emailResponse.json();\n      const primary = emails.find((e: any) => e.primary && e.verified);\n      email = primary?.email ?? null;\n    }\n\n    return {\n      provider: 'github',\n      provider_id: String(data.id),\n      email,\n      name: data.name ?? null,\n      avatar_url: data.avatar_url ?? null,\n    };\n  }\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement Google strategy**\n\nSame pattern using `import { Google } from 'arctic'`. # Auth Implementation Plan Review

**Plan:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-22-auth-implementation.md`
**Spec:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-22-auth-design.md`
**Conventions:** `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/CLAUDE.md`

## What the Plan Does Well

- Comprehensive file structure listing with clear CREATE/MODIFY annotations
- Consistent TDD approach (write failing test, implement, verify)
- Follows the project's 5-step env variable process faithfully
- Migration patterns match spec requirements (partial unique indexes, soft deletes, transactions)
- Good separation of model/service/controller layers
- Encryption utility uses AES-256-GCM with random IV, which is correct
- Auth code pattern and refresh token rotation with theft detection are both present

---

## 1. Spec Coverage

### Critical

**[C1] Missing `@fastify/cookie` in Task 1 dependency list.** Task 16 mentions installing it as a separate step (Step 2), but it should be part of the initial dependency installation in Task 1. This is a runtime dependency that the auth controller depends on. The plan inconsistently handles this -- it is not in the Task 1 install command but appears later as an ad hoc install. This creates a fragile ordering dependency and means Task 1's "verify installation" step will not catch a missing package.

**[C2] Missing refresh token reuse detection logic.** The spec (lines 380-385) explicitly requires that when a previously-rotated refresh token is reused, the entire session must be invalidated. The plan mentions this in the `refreshTokens` description (Task 15, line 1270-1272: "If invalid (previously rotated) -> destroy session (theft detection)"), but the `SessionService` in Task 11 does not include a method for detecting reuse versus simple absence. The `validateRefreshToken` method needs to distinguish between "token never existed" and "token was previously rotated" -- this requires storing rotated token IDs briefly (e.g., in a Redis set or with a tombstone key). Without this, a stolen token reuse and a simple invalid token are indistinguishable, and the security requirement from the spec cannot be fulfilled.

### Major

**[M1] Missing CORS configuration.** The spec (line 367) requires "CORS: restricted to frontend domain." The plan has a `FRONTEND_URL` env var but never mentions configuring CORS in `main.ts` or anywhere else. This should be a dedicated step.

**[M2] Invitation acceptance flow is incomplete.** The spec (lines 227-237) describes two scenarios for invitation acceptance: (a) user is already authenticated, and (b) user is not authenticated (redirect to OAuth with invitation token stored in session state). The plan's Task 20 only has `GET /invitations/accept?token=xxx` as `@Public()`, but the plan does not describe how the invitation token is persisted through the OAuth flow when the user is unauthenticated. The `initiateOAuth` method in Task 15 would need to accept and store an optional `invitation_token` in the Redis OAuth state, and `handleCallback` would need to handle it post-authentication. This cross-cutting concern is not addressed.

**[M3] Session invalidation on membership changes is missing.** The spec (lines 212-216) requires that when a user is removed from a company or a company is deleted, all sessions with that `company_id` must be destroyed. Neither the `CompanyMemberService` (Task 10) nor the `SessionService` (Task 11) includes a `destroySessionsByCompanyId` method or any hook to trigger invalidation on member removal or company deletion.

**[M4] No `oauth-account.service.unit-spec.ts` listed.** The file structure (line 144) does not list a unit test for `OAuthAccountService`, and Task 8 only mentions `oauth-account.model.unit-spec.ts`. For 95% coverage, the service needs testing too. By contrast, `user.service.unit-spec.ts` is listed.

### Minor

**[m1] The `sessions:{user_id}` Redis SET has no TTL management.** The spec defines this set (lines 199-202), and the plan mentions adding `session_id` to it and removing on destroy. However, the set itself has no TTL. Over time, if `destroyAllSessions` is never called, the set accumulates stale entries for expired sessions. The plan should add TTL refresh on the set whenever a session is created/refreshed, or clean stale entries periodically.

**[m2] Missing Swagger decorators on controller methods.** The backend conventions (`backend/CLAUDE.md`, lines 217-219) require `@ApiOperation` and `@ApiResponse` decorators on controllers. The plan's controller code samples omit these entirely.

---

## 2. Task Ordering and Dependencies

### Major

**[M5] Dependency graph has errors in parallel groups.** The plan states (line 1735): "Tasks 7 + 8 + 9 + 10: parallel after Tasks 3 + 5." However:
- Task 8 (OAuth Account) depends on Task 6 (encryption utility) because `OAuthAccountModel` uses `encrypt()`/`decrypt()`. The dependency graph arrow on line 1711 shows Task 8 depending on Task 4 (cache), which is wrong -- it depends on Task 3 (db) and Task 6 (encryption).
- Task 5 (migrations) depends on Task 3 (db module) because you need the Knex connection and knexfile to run migrations. The graph shows Task 5 depending on Task 2, but it also needs Task 3's `knexfile.ts`.

**[M6] Tasks 12, 13, 14 do not actually depend on Task 11 (session).** The plan says "Tasks 12 + 13 + 14: parallel after Task 11." Task 12 (decorators) and Task 14 (strategies) have no dependency on the session module. Only Task 13 (AuthGuard) depends on SessionService. Tasks 12 and 14 could start as soon as their imports are available (Task 2 for strategies, no dependency for decorators). This mischaracterization would slow down a parallel execution plan unnecessarily.

### Minor

**[m3] Task 1 and Task 2 are listed as "sequential (deps before env vars)" but could partially overlap.** The env var constants/validation work in Task 2 does not depend on packages being installed -- only the runtime code does. However, this is a minor optimization and the current ordering is safe.

---

## 3. File Paths

### Major

**[M7] Duplicate `db.constant.ts` location.** The plan creates both `backend/src/modules/db/db.constant.ts` (for connection names, Task 3 Step 1) and `backend/src/constants/db.constant.ts` (for table names, Task 3 Step 2). The spec places both under a single `db/` module or `constants/` directory. The split is reasonable but the plan's file structure listing (line 37) shows `backend/src/modules/db/db.constant.ts` while line 23 shows `backend/src/constants/db.constant.ts`. The plan should clarify which constants go where and ensure import aliases are used correctly. Connection-name constants in `@modules/db/db.constant` and table-name constants in `@constants/db.constant` is a workable split, but the naming collision (`db.constant.ts` in two places) will cause confusion.

**[M8] Missing `redis.constant.ts` in constants directory.** The file structure (line 24) lists `backend/src/constants/redis.constant.ts` for "Redis key prefix + connection name constants," but Task 4 creates `backend/src/modules/cache/cache.constant.ts` instead. These appear to serve the same purpose. The plan should reconcile this -- either use the top-level constants file or the module-level one, not both. The file structure listing will mislead the implementing agent.

---

## 4. Code Patterns and Convention Compliance

### Critical

**[C3] Comments in code samples violate the "NO COMMENTS" rule.** The backend conventions explicitly state: "NO COMMENTS, NO EXPLANATIONS in code (except ESLint disable commands)." Multiple code samples in the plan contain comments:
- Line 1019: `// Check @Public() decorator`
- Line 1151: `// Fetch primary email if not public`
- Line 1321: `// Returns access token in body, sets refresh token as httpOnly cookie`
- Line 1327: `// Reads refresh token from cookie, returns new tokens`
- Lines in migration: `// backend/src/constants/db.constant.ts`

Since this plan is intended for agentic workers that will copy code nearly verbatim, these comments will end up in production code and fail linting. The plan should strip all comments from code samples.

### Major

**[M9] `AuthGuard.canActivate` lacks try/catch around `jwtService.verifyAsync`.** If the JWT is malformed or expired, `verifyAsync` throws. The plan's code (lines 1030-1035) does not wrap this in a try/catch, meaning an unhandled exception will propagate as a 500 instead of a clean 401. This should be:
```typescript
try {
  const payload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<IJwtPayload>(token);
} catch {
  throw new UnauthorizedException();
}
```

**[M10] `GitHubStrategy.getUserProfile` does not handle fetch errors or non-OK responses.** Lines 1146-1168 call `fetch()` without checking `response.ok`. A failed GitHub API call would produce `data.id` as `undefined`, creating a corrupt OAuth account.

**[M11] Cookie registration uses `require()` instead of ESM import.** Line 1361 shows `await app.register(require('@fastify/cookie'))`. The project uses TypeScript with ES module imports. This should use `import fastifyCookie from '@fastify/cookie'` at the top of the file.

### Minor

**[m4] `CurrentUser` decorator casts `request as any`.** Line 935 uses `(request as any).user`. A typed approach using a custom Fastify request interface would be more consistent with the strict TypeScript configuration (`noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature` enabled). This is a minor concern since decorators are in the "relaxed rules" category per ESLint config.

**[m5] `encrypt`/`decrypt` functions return type annotation is missing.** The conventions require "All functions must have explicit return types." The encryption utility code samples on lines 583-598 omit return type annotations. Should be `encrypt(...): string` and `decrypt(...): string`.

---

## 5. Testing Coverage

### Major

**[M12] No unit tests for OAuth strategies (Task 14).** `GitHubStrategy` and `GoogleStrategy` contain non-trivial logic (URL construction, token exchange, profile fetching with email fallback). These have no unit tests planned. For 95% coverage, these need testing with mocked `fetch` calls.

**[M13] No unit tests for controllers (Tasks 16, 18, 19).** The plan creates `AuthController`, `UserController`, and `CompanyController` without any unit tests. While E2E tests cover the HTTP layer, the 95% coverage requirement applies to unit coverage as well (per `backend/CLAUDE.md` line 299: "Unit coverage collects from all `src/**/*.ts`"). Without unit tests, controller logic will drag coverage below the threshold.

**[M14] E2E tests (Task 22) lack specificity.** The E2E test descriptions are vague bullet points without detailed test case lists. For critical flows like OAuth callback handling, account linking, and refresh token rotation with theft detection, the plan should enumerate specific test scenarios to ensure nothing is missed.

### Minor

**[m6] No unit test for `InvitationController`.** Similar to M13, but for the invitation module. The plan lists unit tests for `InvitationModel` and `InvitationService` but not the controller.

---

## 6. Completeness -- Missing Steps and Edge Cases

### Critical

**[C4] No `ThrottlerGuard` adapter for Fastify.** The `@nestjs/throttler` module requires a custom guard for Fastify (it does not work with the default Express-based `ThrottlerGuard`). The plan needs to either create a `FastifyThrottlerGuard` extending `ThrottlerGuard` with overridden `getTracker()` and `getRequestResponse()` methods, or configure the throttler module with Fastify-compatible options. Without this, rate limiting will silently fail or throw errors at runtime.

### Major

**[M15] No `keyv` package in Task 1 dependencies.** The plan installs `@keyv/redis` but not the `keyv` base package. `@keyv/redis` is a storage adapter that requires `keyv` as a peer dependency. The install command on line 166 should include `keyv`.

**[M16] Missing `@fastify/cookie` type declarations.** The plan installs `@fastify/cookie` in Task 16 but does not install `@types/` equivalent or check if types are bundled. Since `request.cookies` is used in the controller, TypeScript needs to know about it.

**[M17] `exchangeCode` in AuthService creates a session but the auth code stores `session_id: null` (line 1259).** The flow is: callback stores auth code with `{ user_id, session_id: null }`, then `exchangeCode` creates the session. This means the auth code does not carry role or company_id information. The `exchangeCode` method needs to determine the user's active company and role from the database before creating the session. This lookup logic is not described in the plan.

**[M18] Plan does not address how a brand-new user (no company membership) gets a session.** After a new user signs up via OAuth, they have no company membership. The spec says `company_id` can be `null` in the session. The plan's `exchangeCode` needs to handle this case, defaulting to `company_id: null` and `role` to... what? The spec does not define a role for users without company membership. The plan should address this explicitly -- likely `role: null` or a default, but the session interface (line 830) types role as `'admin' | 'member'` with no null option.

---

## Summary

| Category | Critical | Major | Minor |
|----------|----------|-------|-------|
| Spec Coverage | 1 (C2) | 4 (M1-M4) | 2 (m1-m2) |
| Task Ordering | 0 | 2 (M5-M6) | 1 (m3) |
| File Paths | 0 | 2 (M7-M8) | 0 |
| Code Patterns | 1 (C3) | 3 (M9-M11) | 2 (m4-m5) |
| Testing | 0 | 3 (M12-M14) | 1 (m6) |
| Completeness | 2 (C1, C4) | 4 (M15-M18) | 0 |
| **Total** | **4** | **18** | **6** |

## Verdict: **Requires Revision**

The plan is thorough in structure and covers most of the spec, but has 4 critical issues that would cause runtime failures or security gaps if implemented as-is:

1. **C1** -- Missing `@fastify/cookie` in initial dependencies
2. **C2** -- Refresh token reuse detection cannot work without tombstone/tracking mechanism
3. **C3** -- Code comments throughout samples will fail the project's linting rules
4. **C4** -- Throttler will not work with Fastify without a custom guard adapter

The 18 major issues include missing CORS setup, incomplete invitation acceptance flow, no session invalidation on membership changes, missing unit tests for strategies and controllers, and a role/company bootstrap problem for new users. These should all be addressed before implementation begins.

**Recommended next steps:**
1. Fix the 4 critical issues first
2. Add missing steps for CORS, invitation-through-OAuth flow, and session invalidation on membership changes
3. Add unit test tasks for strategies, controllers, and `OAuthAccountService`
4. Reconcile the duplicate constant file paths (`db.constant.ts`, `redis.constant.ts` vs `cache.constant.ts`)
5. Strip all comments from code samples
6. Correct the dependency graph arrows and parallelization groups
