connections\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 4: Cache Module (Redis)\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/cache/cache.module.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/cache/cache.service.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/cache/cache.constant.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/cache/types/cache.interface.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/unit-tests/cache.service.unit-spec.ts`\n- Create: `backend/test/cache.module.e2e-spec.ts`\n\n**Reference:** `../recommendations-service/src/modules/cache/` — replicate the full pattern.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create `cache.constant.ts`**\n\nFour exports: `REDIS_READ_CLIENT`, `REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT`, `CACHE_WRITE_MANAGER`, `CACHE_READ_MANAGER`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Create `cache.interface.ts`**\n\nDefine `ConsistencyLevel` enum (`EVENTUAL`, `STRONG`), `CacheError` interface, and `Namespaces` enum (initially `SESSION`, `AUTH_CODE`, `LINKING_TOKEN`, `OAUTH_STATE`).\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Create `cache.module.ts`**\n\nRegister providers:\n- `REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT`: `new Redis(REDIS_URL)` with retry strategy, reconnectOnError for READONLY\n- `REDIS_READ_CLIENT`: `new Redis(REDIS_READ_URL)` with `readOnly: true`\n- `CACHE_WRITE_MANAGER`: `new Keyv({ store: new KeyvRedis(REDIS_URL), namespace, useUnlink: true })`\n- `CACHE_READ_MANAGER`: `new Keyv({ store: new KeyvRedis(REDIS_READ_URL), namespace, useUnlink: true })`\n- `CacheService`\n\nExport all providers.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Create `cache.service.ts`**\n\nImplement following the recommendations-service pattern:\n- `get<T>()` with ConsistencyLevel support and recent-writes tracking\n- `set()` with TTL support\n- `has()`\n- `deleteOne()`\n- `deleteAll()`\n- `incrementByOne()`\n- `withLock<T>()` (Redlock wrapper)\n- `acquireLock()` / `releaseLock()`\n- Health monitoring on read replica with automatic fallback\n- `shouldReadFromMaster()` logic\n\nAll functions must have explicit return types. No comments in code.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Write unit tests for CacheService**\n\nFile: `backend/src/unit-tests/cache.service.unit-spec.ts`\n\nTest all methods with mocked Redis clients. Test fallback when replica unhealthy. Test set/get, delete, lock acquisition.\n\n- [ ] **Step 8: Run E2E test**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:e2e -- --testPathPattern cache.module`\nExpected: PASS (requires local Redis)\n\n- [ ] **Step 9: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/src/modules/cache/ backend/src/unit-tests/cache.service.unit-spec.ts backend/test/cache.module.e2e-spec.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat: add cache module with Redis read/write separation, Keyv, and Redlock\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 5: Database Migrations\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/src/migrations/20260322120000_create_users_table.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/migrations/20260322120001_create_companies_table.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/migrations/20260322120002_create_company_members_table.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/migrations/20260322120003_create_oauth_accounts_table.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/migrations/20260322120004_create_invitations_table.ts`\n\n**Reference:** `../recommendations-service/src/migrations/` for the migration pattern (Logger, transactions, idempotent checks, table name constants).\n\nUse PostgreSQL types: `uuid`, `varchar`, `text`, `timestamp with time zone`. All migrations wrapped in `knex.transaction()`. No comments in migration code.\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create users table migration**\n\nColumns: `_id` (uuid PK), `email` (varchar(255) unique not null), `name` (varchar(255) nullable), `avatar_url` (varchar(512) nullable), `last_login_at` (timestamptz nullable), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `deleted_at`. Index on `(email, deleted_at)`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Create companies table migration**\n\nColumns: `_id` (uuid PK), `name` (varchar(255) not null), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `deleted_at`.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Create company_members table migration**\n\nColumns per spec. Add:\n- Partial unique: `UNIQUE(user_id, company_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`\n- Partial unique: `UNIQUE(company_id) WHERE role = 'admin' AND deleted_at IS NULL` (one admin per company)\n- Foreign keys to users and companies with `ON DELETE CASCADE`\n\nUse `knex.raw()` for partial unique indexes.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Create oauth_accounts table migration**\n\nColumns per spec. Add:\n- Partial unique: `UNIQUE(provider, provider_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`\n- Foreign key to users with `ON DELETE CASCADE`\n- Index on `(user_id, deleted_at)`\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Create invitations table migration**\n\nColumns per spec. Add:\n- Partial unique: `UNIQUE(company_id, email) WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL`\n- Foreign keys to companies and users (invited_by) with `ON DELETE CASCADE`\n- Index on `(token)`\n- Index on `(company_id, deleted_at)`\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Run migrations**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run migrate`\nExpected: All 5 migrations applied\n\n- [ ] **Step 7: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/src/migrations/\ngit commit -m \"feat: add database migrations for users, companies, company_members, oauth_accounts, invitations\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 6: Encryption Utility\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/src/utils/encryption.util.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/unit-tests/encryption.util.unit-spec.ts`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Write failing test**\n\nFile: `backend/src/unit-tests/encryption.util.unit-spec.ts`\n\nTest cases:\n- encrypt + decrypt returns original value\n- Two encryptions of same plaintext produce different ciphertexts (random IV)\n- Decrypt of garbage throws\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern encryption`\nExpected: FAIL\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Implement encryption utility**\n\nFile: `backend/src/utils/encryption.util.ts`\n\nAES-256-GCM with random 16-byte IV. `backend/src/modules/session/types/session.interface.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/session/session.service.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/session/session.module.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/unit-tests/session.service.unit-spec.ts`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create session interfaces**\n\n```\nISession: user_id, session_id, role ('admin' | 'member' | null), company_id (string | null), provider, ip, user_agent, created_at, last_active\nIRefreshToken: user_id, session_id\n```\n\nNote: `role` can be `null` for new users with no company membership yet.\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Write failing unit tests for SessionService**\n\nTest methods:\n- `createSession({ session })` — stores session + adds to user's session set + creates refresh token. Set also has a TTL matching the session.\n- `getSession({ userId, sessionId })` — retrieves session from Redis\n- `updateLastActive({ userId, sessionId })`\n- `destroySession({ userId, sessionId })` — deletes session + refresh token + removes from set\n- `destroyAllSessions({ userId })` — deletes all sessions via the set\n- `destroySessionsByCompanyId({ userId, companyId })` — finds sessions with matching company_id, destroys them\n- `validateRefreshToken({ refreshToken })` — returns session reference or null\n- `rotateRefreshToken({ oldToken, session })` — stores tombstone for old token (TTL: 1 hour), creates new token. Returns new token UUID.\n- `detectRefreshTokenReuse({ refreshToken })` — checks if a tombstone exists for this token. If yes, returns the session reference (for invalidation). This enables the theft detection per spec.\n- `updateCompanyContext({ userId, sessionId, companyId, role })` — for company switching\n\nMock ioredis clients.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern session`\nExpected: FAIL\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Implement SessionService**\n\nInject `REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT` and `REDIS_READ_CLIENT` directly.\n\nKey patterns:\n- Session key: `session:{user_id}:{session_id}`\n- Session set: `sessions:{user_id}` (with TTL refreshed on session create)\n- Refresh key: `refresh:{token_uuid}`\n- Refresh tombstone: `refresh_tombstone:{token_uuid}` (TTL: 1 hour, value: `{ user_id, session_id }`)\n- All writes go to write client\n- Session reads use read client with fallback\n- TTL: `JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS * 86400` seconds\n- Use Redis `MULTI/EXEC` for atomic session creation\n- JSON serialize/deserialize session values\n\nRefresh token reuse detection flow:\n1. `validateRefreshToken` checks `refresh:{token}` — if found, valid token\n2. If not found, `detectRefreshTokenReuse` checks `refresh_tombstone:{token}` — if found, this was a previously-rotated token (theft detected)\n3. On theft detection, caller destroys the entire session\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern session`\nExpected: PASS\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Create SessionModule**\n\nImport `CacheModule` (for Redis client access). Also tests theft detection path.\n- `linkAccount({ linkingToken, userId })` → validates token, creates oauth_account\n- `createCompany({ name, userId })` → creates company + company_member (admin) in Knex transaction, updates session\n- `logout({ userId, sessionId })`\n- `logoutAll({ userId })`\n\nMock all dependencies.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Implement AuthService**\n\nKey implementation details:\n\n**initiateOAuth:** Generate state via `randomBytes(32).toString('hex')`, codeVerifier via Arctic's `generateCodeVerifier()`. Store in Redis: key `oauth_state:{state}`, value `{ codeVerifier, provider, invitationToken? }`, TTL 10min. Return authorization URL.\n\n**handleCallback:** Retrieve + delete `oauth_state:{state}`. Exchange code via strategy. Fetch profile. Account resolution: (1) find by provider+id → auth code, (2) find by email → linking token, (3) no match → create user + oauth_account + auth code. If invitationToken was in state, process invitation acceptance after user creation/login.\n\n**exchangeCode:** Retrieve + delete `auth_code:{code}`. Look up user's company memberships via `companyMemberService.findByUserId()`. Pick first membership for initial context (or null). Create session with `{ role, company_id }` from membership (or `{ role: null, company_id: null }` for new users). Sign JWT. Return tokens.\n\n**refreshTokens:** Call `sessionService.validateRefreshToken()`. If null, call `sessionService.detectRefreshTokenReuse()`. If reuse detected → destroy session, log security event, throw UnauthorizedException. If valid → rotate token, sign new JWT. Return tokens.\n\n**createCompany:** Knex transaction: insert company, insert company_member (role: admin). Update session company context.\n\n- [ ] **Step 5: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd backend && npm run test:unit -- --testPathPattern auth.service`\nExpected: PASS\n\n- [ ] **Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts backend/src/modules/auth/dto/ backend/src/unit-tests/auth.service.unit-spec.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat: add auth service with OAuth orchestration, token exchange, linking, and theft detection\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## Task 16: Auth Controller\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts`\n- Create: `backend/src/unit-tests/auth.controller.unit-spec.ts`\n- Modify: `backend/src/main.ts`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Register @fastify/cookie in main.ts**\n\nAdd `import fastifyCookie from '@fastify/cookie'` at top.      1→# Authentication & Authorization Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Implement OAuth-based authentication (GitHub + Google) with JWT sessions backed by Redis, multi-tenant company model with admin/member roles, and PostgreSQL persistence via Knex.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Arctic handles OAuth flows for GitHub and Google with PKCE. JWTs (HS256, 1hr) are issued after a secure code exchange. Sessions and refresh tokens live in Redis with read/write separation. PostgreSQL stores users, OAuth accounts, companies, memberships, and invitations via Knex with read/write replicas. All patterns follow the existing recommendations-service codebase.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** NestJS 11, Fastify, Arctic, @nestjs/jwt, Knex + nest-knexjs + pg, ioredis + keyv + @keyv/redis + redlock, @nestjs/throttler, @fastify/cookie, class-validator, class-transformer
    10→
    11→**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-22-auth-design.md`
    12→
    13→**Reference codebase:** `../recommendations-service/` — replicate Knex and Redis patterns from there.

    17→- NO relative imports — use `@aliases` only
    18→- ALL functions must have explicit return types
    19→- ALL custom functions must use single object destructuring parameter

**Status: DONE_WITH_CONCERNS**

All 4 steps completed successfully. Commit `c30980e` created.

**Installed versions:**
- `arctic@3.7.0`, `@nestjs/jwt@11.0.2`, `knex@3.2.0`, `nest-knexjs@0.0.34`, `pg@8.20.0`, `ioredis@5.10.1`, `keyv@5.6.0`, `@keyv/redis@5.1.6`, `redlock@5.0.0-beta.2`, `@nestjs/throttler@6.5.0`, `@fastify/cookie@11.0.2`, `@types/pg` (dev)

**Concern — peer dependency conflict resolved with `--legacy-peer-deps`:**

The plain `npm install` failed because `@keyv/redis@5.1.6` requires `keyv@^5.6.0` as a peer, but existing tooling deps (eslint, got via `cacheable-request`) locked `keyv@4.5.4` in the tree. All `@keyv/redis` versions 4.0+ carry a `keyv@^5.x` requirement, so there is no version that avoids this.

The fix used `--legacy-peer-deps`, which allows npm to install both `keyv@4.5.4` (for tooling) and `keyv@5.6.0` (for `@keyv/redis`) as separate nodes in the dependency tree. This is functionally safe — the two versions don't conflict at runtime because they serve different packages. However, the `package-lock.json` now carries this flag implicitly for this resolution, and future `npm install` runs on this project may require `--legacy-peer-deps` as well (or an `.npmrc` setting).

    99→
   100→	@IsString()
   101→	@IsNotEmpty()
   102→	FRONTEND_URL: string;
   103→
   104→	@IsString()
   105→	@Length(64, 64)
   106→	ENCRYPTION_KEY: string;
   107→}
   108→
   109→export function validate({
   110→	envConfig,
   111→}: {
   112→	envConfig: Record<string, unknown>;
   113→}): EnvironmentVariables {
   114→	const validatedConfig = plainToInstance(EnvironmentVariables, envConfig, {
   115→		enableImplicitConversion: true,
   116→	});
   117→
   118→	const errors = validateSync(validatedConfig, {
   119→		skipMissingProperties: false,
   120→		forbidUnknownValues: true,
   121→		stopAtFirstError: true,
   122→		forbidNonWhitelisted: true,
   123→	});
   124→
   125→	if (errors.length > 0) {
   126→		Logger.error('Config validation error', errors);
   127→		throw new Error(errors.toString());
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	return validatedConfig;
   131→}
   132→

Key differences:\n\n- Use env var constants `REDIS_URL` and `REDIS_READ_URL` from `@constants/env-variables.constant`\n- Use `getEnv()` from `@utils/get-env-variable.util` to read env vars\n- Register ioredis clients with retry strategy and reconnect logic\n- Register Keyv instances with `@keyv/redis` adapter\n- Namespace: `${SERVER_NAME}:${NODE_ENV}`\n- Module should be `@Global()`\n- Provide and export: `REDIS_READ_CLIENT`, `REDIS_WRITE_CLIENT`, `CACHE_WRITE_MANAGER`, `CACHE_READ_MANAGER`, `CacheService`\n\n### Step 4: Create cache.service.ts\n\nRead the reference service at `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/src/modules/cache/cache.service.ts` and replicate the pattern. **Key decisions / deviations from the reference:**

- `@nestjs/cache-manager` is not installed in this project. The module uses `getEnv()` directly in factory functions (same pattern as `db.module.ts`) instead of `EnvConfigService` DI + `CM.registerAsync`.

- The `redlock` package uses `"type": "module"` with a CJS build, requiring `{ __esModule: true, default: ... }` in the Jest mock to avoid the "not a constructor" error.
- Interface types live at `@modules/cache/types/cache.interface.ts` (not `@interfaces/`) since the task spec places them inside the module.
- `deleteAll` takes `{ namespace }` per the task spec (the reference takes no params).

{

  "content": "---\nname: Auth system design decisions\ndescription: Key auth architecture decisions for the no-name-proj B2B multi-tenant app\ntype: project\n---\n\nMulti-tenant B2B app with OAuth auth (GitHub + Google via Arctic library).\n\nKey decisions:\n- **Database**: PostgreSQL with read/write replicas via Knex.js (nest-knexjs), NOT an ORM\n- **Cache/Sessions**: Redis with read/write separation via ioredis, Keyv, Redlock — pattern replicated from ../recommendations-service/\n- **Auth flow**: Arctic OAuth with PKCE → temporary auth code in Redis → POST /auth/token exchange → JWT (HS256, 1hr access + 30d refresh in Redis)\n- **Session**: Server-side in Redis, validated on every request. Refresh token theft detection via tombstone keys.\n- **Roles**: Derived from company_members table (not stored on users). Admin 1:1 with company (DB-enforced partial unique index). Members can belong to multiple companies.\n- **Account linking**: Prompt user when email conflict detected (not auto-link)\n- **Invitations**: Admin invites by email only. Role restricted to 'member'. 72hr expiry, single-use tokens.\n\n**Why:** Patterns replicate ../recommendations-service/ for consistency across the team's projects.\n\n**How to apply:** All new modules follow the same Knex model/service/DTO pattern. Redis operations use the cache module's read/write separation.\n"
}

Valid JWT + session exists → attach IRequestUser to request, return true\n\nMock: JwtService (verifyAsync), SessionService (getSession), Reflector (getAllAndOverride).\n\nCreate a mock ExecutionContext that returns a Fastify-like request object.\n\n### Step 2: Implement AuthGuard\n\nFile: `src/modules/auth/guards/auth.guard.ts`\n\n```typescript\n@Injectable()\nexport class AuthGuard implements CanActivate {\n\tprivate readonly logger = new Logger(AuthGuard.name);\n\n\tconstructor(\n\t\tprivate readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n\t\tprivate readonly sessionService: SessionService,\n\t\tprivate readonly reflector: Reflector,\n\t) {}\n\n\tasync canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n\t\tconst isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n\t\t\tcontext.getHandler(),\n\t\t\tcontext.getClass(),\n\t\t]);\n\t\tif (isPublic) return true;\n\n\t\tconst request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();\n\t\tconst token = this.extractTokenFromHeader(request);\n\t\tif (!token) throw new UnauthorizedException();\n\n\t\tlet payload: IJwtPayload;\n\t\ttry {\n\t\t\tpayload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<IJwtPayload>(token);\n\t\t} catch {\n\t\t\tthrow new UnauthorizedException();\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tconst session = await this.sessionService.getSession({\n\t\t\tuserId: payload.user_id,\n\t\t\tsessionId: payload.session_id,\n\t\t});\n\t\tif (!session) throw new UnauthorizedException();\n\n\t\trequest.user = {\n\t\t\tuser_id: payload.user_id,\n\t\t\tsession_id: payload.session_id,\n\t\t\trole: session.role,\n\t\t\tcompany_id: session.company_id,\n\t\t} satisfies IRequestUser;\n\n\t\treturn true;\n\t}\n\n\tprivate extractTokenFromHeader(request: any): string | null {\n\t\tconst [type, token] = request.headers?.authorization?.split(' ') ?? [];\n\t\treturn type === 'Bearer' ? token ?? null : null;\n\t}\n}\n```\n\nNote: `extractTokenFromHeader` is a private helper, not a custom function — it doesn't need object destructuring.\n\n### Step 3: Run AuthGuard tests\nRun: `npm run test:unit -- --testPathPatterns auth.guard`\nExpected: PASS\n\n### Step 4: Write failing tests for RolesGuard\n\nFile: `src/unit-tests/roles.guard.unit-spec.ts`\n\nTests:\n- No roles metadata → return true (allow all)\n- User role matches required roles → return true\n- User role not in required roles → throw ForbiddenException\n- User role is null (no company) → throw ForbiddenException\n- No user on request → throw ForbiddenException\n\n### Step 5: Implement RolesGuard\n\nFile: `src/modules/auth/guards/roles.guard.ts`\n\nUses Reflector to read ROLES_KEY metadata. **Status: DONE**

All steps completed successfully.

**Summary:**

- `src/modules/auth/guards/auth.guard.ts` — `AuthGuard` implementing `CanActivate`. Checks `IS_PUBLIC_KEY` metadata via Reflector, extracts Bearer token from Authorization header, verifies JWT with `JwtService`, validates session exists in Redis via `SessionService`, attaches `IRequestUser` to `request.user`. Logger is used in the catch block (`warn` on JWT verification failure).

- `src/modules/auth/guards/roles.guard.ts` — `RolesGuard` implementing `CanActivate`. Reads `ROLES_KEY` metadata; returns true if no roles required; throws `ForbiddenException` if no user, null role, or role not in required set.

- `src/modules/auth/guards/fastify-throttler.guard.ts` — `FastifyThrottlerGuard` extending `ThrottlerGuard`, overriding `getRequestResponse` to use Fastify's request/response objects via `switchToHttp()`.

- `src/unit-tests/auth.guard.unit-spec.ts` — 8 tests covering: public routes, missing header, non-Bearer auth, invalid JWT, session not found, valid JWT+session (user attachment and `getSession` call args).

- `src/unit-tests/roles.guard.unit-spec.ts` — 8 tests covering: no metadata, empty roles array, matching role, member in multi-role array, role mismatch, null role, and no user on request.

**One deviation from spec:** The `catch` block in `AuthGuard` uses `catch (error)` rather than bare `catch {}` in order to pass `error` to `this.logger.warn(...)` — keeping the logger used and satisfying `noUnusedLocals`. This is strictly better than the bare `catch` in the spec.

     1→import {
     2→	CanActivate,
     3→	ExecutionContext,
     4→	Injectable,
     5→	Logger,
     6→	UnauthorizedException,
     7→} from '@nestjs/common';
     8→import { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';
     9→import { JwtService } from '@nestjs/jwt';
    10→
    11→import { IS_PUBLIC_KEY } from '@modules/auth/decorators/public.decorator';
    12→import { IJwtPayload, IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';
    13→import { SessionService } from '@modules/session/session.service';
    14→
    15→@Injectable()
    16→export class AuthGuard implements CanActivate {
    17→	private readonly logger = new Logger(AuthGuard.name);
    18→
    19→	constructor(
    20→		private readonly jwtService: JwtService,
    21→		private readonly sessionService: SessionService,
    22→		private readonly reflector: Reflector,
    23→	) {}
    24→
    25→	async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {
    26→		const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [
    27→			context.getHandler(),
    28→			context.getClass(),
    29→		]);
    30→		if (isPublic) return true;
    31→
    32→		const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    33→		const token = this.extractTokenFromHeader(request);
    34→		if (!token) throw new UnauthorizedException();
    35→
    36→		let payload: IJwtPayload;
    37→		try {
    38→			payload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<IJwtPayload>(token);
    39→		} catch (error) {
    40→			this.logger.warn('JWT verification failed', { error });
    41→			throw new UnauthorizedException();
    42→		}
    43→
    44→		const session = await this.sessionService.getSession({
    45→			userId: payload.user_id,
    46→			sessionId: payload.session_id,
    47→		});
    48→		if (!session) throw new UnauthorizedException();
    49→
    50→		request.user = {
    51→			user_id: payload.user_id,
    52→			session_id: payload.session_id,
    53→			role: session.role,
    54→			company_id: session.company_id,
    55→		} satisfies IRequestUser;
    56→
    57→		return true;
    58→	}
    59→
    60→	private extractTokenFromHeader(request: any): string | null {
    61→		const [type, token] = request.headers?.authorization?.split(' ') ?? [];
    62→		return type === 'Bearer' ? (token ?? null) : null;
    63→	}
    64→}
    65→

     1→import {
     2→	CanActivate,
     3→	ExecutionContext,
     4→	ForbiddenException,
     5→	Injectable,
     6→} from '@nestjs/common';
     7→import { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';
     8→
     9→import { ROLES_KEY } from '@modules/auth/decorators/roles.decorator';
    10→import { IRequestUser } from '@modules/auth/types/auth.interface';
    11→
    12→@Injectable()
    13→export class RolesGuard implements CanActivate {
    14→	constructor(private readonly reflector: Reflector) {}
    15→
    16→	canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): boolean {
    17→		const requiredRoles = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<('admin' | 'member')[]>(
    18→			ROLES_KEY,
    19→			[context.getHandler(), context.getClass()],
    20→		);
    21→
    22→		if (!requiredRoles || requiredRoles.length === 0) return true;
    23→
    24→		const request = context.switchToHttp().getRequest();
    25→		const user: IRequestUser | undefined = request.user;
    26→
    27→		if (!user) throw new ForbiddenException();
    28→		if (!user.role) throw new ForbiddenException();
    29→		if (!requiredRoles.includes(user.role)) throw new ForbiddenException();
    30→
    31→		return true;
    32→	}
    33→}
    34→

If user found → linking conflict, generate linking token\n  5. If no user → create new user + oauth_account, generate auth code\n- Auth code: store in Redis `auth_code:${code}` with JSON `{ user_id }`, TTL from AUTH_CODE_TTL\n- Linking token: store in Redis `linking_token:${token}` with JSON ILinkingToken, TTL from LINKING_TOKEN_TTL\n- Return appropriate result\n\n**exchangeCode({ code, ip, userAgent }):**\n- Get + delete `auth_code:${code}` from Redis\n- If not found, throw UnauthorizedException\n- Look up user's company memberships via companyMemberService.findByUserId()\n- Pick first membership for initial context (or null if none)\n- Generate sessionId via randomUUID()\n- Generate refreshToken via randomUUID()\n- Create session via sessionService.createSession()\n- Sign JWT with IJwtPayload via jwtService.signAsync()\n- Return { accessToken, refreshToken }\n\n**refreshTokens({ refreshToken, ip, userAgent }):**\n- Call sessionService.validateRefreshToken({ refreshToken })\n- If null → call sessionService.detectRefreshTokenReuse({ refreshToken })\n  - If reuse detected → destroy session, throw UnauthorizedException\n  - If not → throw UnauthorizedException\n- Get session to read current role/company_id\n- Rotate refresh token via sessionService.rotateRefreshToken()\n- Update last_active\n- Sign new JWT\n- Return { accessToken, refreshToken: newToken }\n\n**linkAccount({ linkingToken, userId }):**\n- Get + delete `linking_token:${linkingToken}` from Redis\n- Verify userId matches existing_user_id in the token data\n- Create oauth_account via oauthAccountService.create()\n\n**createCompany({ name, userId, sessionId }):**\n- Create company via companyService (you'll need to inject CompanyService too)\n- Create company_member with role 'admin' via companyMemberService.create()\n- Update session company context via sessionService.updateCompanyContext()\n- Return { company_id }\n\n**logout({ userId, sessionId }):**\n- sessionService.destroySession({ userId, sessionId })\n\n**logoutAll({ userId }):**\n- sessionService.destroyAllSessions({ userId })\n\n### Step 4: Run tests\nRun: `npm run test:unit -- --testPathPatterns auth.service`\nExpected: PASS\n\n### Step 5: Run Prettier\n```bash\nnpx prettier --write src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts src/modules/auth/dto/ src/unit-tests/auth.service.unit-spec.ts\n```\n\n### Step 6: Commit\n```bash\ngit add src/modules/auth/auth.service.ts src/modules/auth/dto/ src/unit-tests/auth.service.unit-spec.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat: add auth service with OAuth orchestration, token exchange, linking, and theft detection\"\n```\n\nReport status: DONE, DONE_WITH_CONCERNS, NEEDS_CONTEXT, or BLOCKED"
}

Here is a summary of what was implemented:

**Files created:**
- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/auth/dto/token-exchange.dto.ts`
- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/auth/dto/link-account.dto.ts`

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/unit-tests/auth.service.unit-spec.ts`

**Key implementation decisions:**

1. `REDIS_READ_CLIENT` not injected — `AuthService` only writes/reads via `getdel` (atomic get+delete for single-use tokens), which must go through the write client. No separate read needed here.

2. `IAuthCode` stores both `user_id` and `session_id` (UUID generated at callback time, before session creation), so `exchangeCode` can create the session with a deterministic session ID.

3. `refreshTokens` type signature still accepts `ip`/`userAgent` in the parameter object (for future use / controller compatibility) but does not destructure them — avoids unused-variable TS errors while keeping the API surface consistent with what the controller will call.

4. `expiresIn` cast to `StringValue` (from `ms` package) to satisfy the strict `@types/jsonwebtoken` overload.

5. All 19 unit tests pass, covering all 4 `handleCallback` branches, token reuse theft detection, zero-membership session context, and all CRUD paths.

   207→	async exchangeCode({
   208→		code,
   209→		ip,
   210→		userAgent,
   211→		provider,
   212→	}: {
   213→		code: string;
   214→		ip: string;

   216→		provider: 'github' | 'google';
   217→	}): Promise<{ accessToken: string; refreshToken: string }> {
   218→		this.logger.log('Exchanging auth code for tokens');
   219→
   220→		const raw = await this.redisWrite.getdel(`auth_code:${code}`);
   221→
   222→		if (!raw) {
   223→			throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid or expired auth code');
   224→		}
   225→
   226→		const authCodeData = JSON.parse(raw) as IAuthCode;
   227→		const { user_id, session_id } = authCodeData;
   228→
   229→		const memberships = await this.companyMemberService.findByUserId({ userId: user_id });
   230→		const firstMembership = memberships[0] ?? null;
   231→
   232→		const refreshToken = randomUUID();
   233→		const now = new Date().toISOString();
   234→
   235→		const session: ISession = {
   236→			user_id,
   237→			session_id,
   238→			role: firstMembership ? firstMembership.role : null,
   239→			company_id: firstMembership ? firstMembership.company_id : null,
   240→			provider,
   241→			ip,
   242→			user_agent: userAgent,
   243→			created_at: now,
   244→			last_active: now,
   245→		};
   246→
   247→		await this.sessionService.createSession({ session, refreshToken });
   248→
   249→		const expiry = this.configService.get<string>({ key: JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY });
   250→
   251→		const payload: IJwtPayload = {
   252→			user_id,
   253→			session_id,
   254→			role: session.role,
   255→			company_id: session.company_id,
   256→		};
   257→
   258→		const accessToken = await this.jwtService.signAsync(payload, {
   259→			expiresIn: expiry as StringValue,
   260→		});
   261→
   262→		return { accessToken, refreshToken };
   263→	}
   264→
   265→	async refreshTokens({
   266→		refreshToken,
   267→	}: {
   268→		refreshToken: string;
   269→		ip: string;
   270→		userAgent: string;
   271→	}): Promise<{ accessToken: string; refreshToken: string }> {
   272→		this.logger.log('Refreshing tokens');
   273→
   274→		const tokenData = await this.sessionService.validateRefreshToken({ refreshToken });
   275→
   276→		if (!tokenData) {
   277→			const reuseData = await this.sessionService.detectRefreshTokenReuse({
   278→				refreshToken,
   279→			});
   280→
   281→			if (reuseData) {
   282→				await this.sessionService.destroySession({
   283→					userId: reuseData.user_id,
   284→					sessionId: reuseData.session_id,
   285→				});
   286→			}
   287→
   288→			throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid or expired refresh token');
   289→		}
   290→
   291→		const { user_id, session_id } = tokenData;
   292→
   293→		const session = await this.sessionService.getSession({
   294→			userId: user_id,
   295→			sessionId: session_id,
   296→		});
   297→
   298→		if (!session) {
   299→			throw new UnauthorizedException('Session not found');
   300→		}
   301→
   302→		const newRefreshToken = await this.sessionService.rotateRefreshToken({
   303→			oldToken: refreshToken,
   304→			session,
   305→		});
   306→
   307→		await this.sessionService.updateLastActive({
   308→			userId: user_id,
   309→			sessionId: session_id,
   310→		});
   311→
   312→		const expiry = this.configService.get<string>({ key: JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY });
   313→
   314→		const payload: IJwtPayload = {
   315→			user_id,
   316→			session_id,
   317→			role: session.role,
   318→			company_id: session.company_id,
   319→		};
   320→
   321→		const accessToken = await this.jwtService.signAsync(payload, {
   322→			expiresIn: expiry as StringValue,
   323→		});
   324→
   325→		return { accessToken, refreshToken: newRefreshToken };
   326→	}
   327→
   328→	async linkAccount({
   329→		linkingToken,
   330→		userId,
   331→	}: {
   332→		linkingToken: string;
   333→		userId: string;
   334→	}): Promise<void> {
   335→		this.logger.log('Linking OAuth account', { userId });
   336→
   337→		const raw = await this.redisWrite.getdel(`linking_token:${linkingToken}`);
   338→
   339→		if (!raw) {

   341→		}

   388→		return { company_id: company._id };
   389→	}

   399→		await this.sessionService.destroySession({ userId, sessionId });

FRONTEND_URL:\n  ```typescript\n  app.enableCors({\n    origin: getEnv<string>({ key: FRONTEND_URL }),\n    credentials: true,\n  });\n  ```\n  Import `FRONTEND_URL` from `src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts`\n\n```typescript\n@ApiTags('Auth')\n@Controller('auth')\nexport class AuthController {\n  constructor(private readonly authService: AuthService) {}\n```\n\nEndpoints:\n\n**GET /auth/github** — `@Public()`, redirect to GitHub OAuth\n```typescript\n@Public()\n@Get('github')\n@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Initiate GitHub OAuth' })\n@ApiResponse({ status: 302, description: 'Redirect to GitHub' })\nasync githubAuth(@Res() reply: FastifyReply): Promise<void> {\n  const url = await this.authService.initiateOAuth({ provider: 'github' });\n  reply.redirect(url.toString());\n}\n```\n\n**GET /auth/github/callback** — `@Public()`, handle GitHub callback\n```typescript\n@Public()\n@Get('github/callback')\nasync githubCallback(\n  @Query('code') code: string,\n  @Query('state') state: string,\n  @Res() reply: FastifyReply,\n): Promise<void> {\n  // call authService.handleCallback, redirect to frontend with result\n  const result = await this.authService.handleCallback({ provider: 'github', code, state });\n  const frontendUrl = getEnv<string>({ key: FRONTEND_URL });\n  if (result.type === 'auth_code') {\n    reply.redirect(`${frontendUrl}/auth/callback?code=${result.code}`);\n  } else {\n    reply.redirect(`${frontendUrl}/auth/link?linking_token=${result.linkingToken}`);\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**GET /auth/google** and **GET /auth/google/callback** — same pattern for Google\n\n**POST /auth/token** — `@Public()`, exchange auth code for JWT\n```typescript\n@Public()\n@Post('token')\nasync exchangeToken(\n  @Body() dto: TokenExchangeDto,\n  @Req() request: FastifyRequest,\n  @Res() reply: FastifyReply,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const result = await this.authService.exchangeCode({\n    code: dto.code,\n    ip: request.ip,\n    userAgent: request.headers['user-agent'] || '',\n    provider: 'github', // TODO: include provider in auth code\n  });\n\n  reply.setCookie(REFRESH_TOKEN_COOKIE_NAME, result.refreshToken, {\n    httpOnly: true,\n    secure: !isDevEnv(),\n    sameSite: 'strict',\n    path: '/api/auth',\n    maxAge: 30 * 24 * 60 * 60,\n  });\n\n  reply.send({ access_token: result.accessToken });\n}\n```\n\nImport `isDevEnv` from `@utils/common.util`.\n\n**POST /auth/refresh** — `@Public()`, refresh tokens from cookie\n```typescript\n@Public()\n@Post('refresh')\nasync refresh(\n  @Req() request: FastifyRequest,\n  @Res() reply: FastifyReply,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const refreshToken = (request.cookies as Record<string, string>)[REFRESH_TOKEN_COOKIE_NAME];\n  if (!refreshToken) throw new UnauthorizedException('No refresh token');\n\n  const result = await this.authService.refreshTokens({ refreshToken });\n\n  reply.setCookie(REFRESH_TOKEN_COOKIE_NAME, result.refreshToken, {\n    httpOnly: true,\n    secure: !isDevEnv(),\n    sameSite: 'strict',\n    path: '/api/auth',\n    maxAge: 30 * 24 * 60 * 60,\n  });\n\n  reply.send({ access_token: result.accessToken });\n}\n```\n\n**POST /auth/logout** — requires auth\n```typescript\n@Post('logout')\nasync logout(@CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser): Promise<{ success: boolean }> {\n  await this.authService.logout({ userId: user.user_id, sessionId: user.session_id });\n  return { success: true };\n}\n```\n\n**POST /auth/logout/all** — requires auth\n```typescript\n@Post('logout/all')\nasync logoutAll(@CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser): Promise<{ success: boolean }> {\n  await this.authService.logoutAll({ userId: user.user_id });\n  return { success: true };\n}\n```\n\n**POST /auth/link** — requires auth\n```typescript\n@Post('link')\nasync linkAccount(\n  @Body() dto: LinkAccountDto,\n  @CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser,\n): Promise<{ success: boolean }> {\n  await this.authService.linkAccount({ linkingToken: dto.linking_token, userId: user.user_id });\n  return { success: true };\n}\n```\n\n**POST /auth/signup/company** — requires auth\n```typescript\n@Post('signup/company')\nasync createCompany(\n  @Body() dto: CreateCompanyDto,\n  @CurrentUser() user: IRequestUser,\n): Promise<{ company_id: string }> {\n  return this.authService.createCompany({\n    name: dto.name,\n    userId: user.user_id,\n    sessionId: user.session_id,\n  });\n}\n```\n\nAdd `@ApiOperation` and `@ApiResponse` to ALL methods.\n\n### Step ---

**Files modified/created:**

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/main.ts` — Added `import fastifyCookie from '@fastify/cookie'` and `await app.register(fastifyCookie)`. Changed CORS `origin: true` to `getEnv<string>({ key: FRONTEND_URL })`. Imported `FRONTEND_URL` from `@constants/env-variables.constant`.

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts` — New file. Implements `AuthController` with 10 endpoints: `GET /auth/github`, `GET /auth/github/callback`, `GET /auth/google`, `GET /auth/google/callback`, `POST /auth/token`, `POST /auth/refresh`, `POST /auth/logout`, `POST /auth/logout/all`, `POST /auth/link`, `POST /auth/signup/company`. Uses `@Public()` on unauthenticated routes, `@CurrentUser()` on authenticated routes. Sets/reads refresh token via HttpOnly cookies. Includes `@ApiOperation` and `@ApiResponse` on all methods. Side-effect imports `@fastify/cookie` to activate its Fastify type augmentation (`setCookie`, `cookies`).

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/unit-tests/auth.controller.unit-spec.ts` — 15 unit tests covering all 10 endpoints. Mocks `AuthService`, `arctic` (ESM interop), `@fastify/cookie`, `getEnv`, and `isDevEnv`. Verifies cookie setting on token exchange/refresh, cookie reading on refresh, `UnauthorizedException` when no cookie present, and correct delegation to `AuthService` for all endpoints.

**Notable concern:** The `arctic` ESM package must be mocked in any test that transitively imports `auth.service.ts`. The controller spec adds `jest.mock('arctic', ...)` to handle this — consistent with the existing `auth.service.unit-spec.ts` pattern.

    27→	const globalPrefix = getEnv<string>({ key: GLOBAL_PREFIX });
    28→	const nodeEnv = getEnv<Environment>({ key: NODE_ENV });
    29→	const isDev = nodeEnv === Environment.DEVELOPMENT;
    30→
    31→	const fastifyAdapter = new FastifyAdapter({ trustProxy: true });
    32→
    33→	const app = await NestFactory.create<NestFastifyApplication>(
    34→		AppModule,
    35→		fastifyAdapter,
    36→		{
    37→			logger: isDev
    38→				? ['log', 'error', 'warn', 'fatal', 'debug', 'verbose']
    39→				: ['log', 'error', 'warn', 'fatal'],
    40→		},
    41→	);
    42→
    43→	await app.register(fastifyCookie);
    44→
    45→	app.enableCors({
    46→		origin: getEnv<string>({ key: FRONTEND_URL }),
    47→		credentials: true,
    48→	});
    49→
    50→	app.useGlobalPipes(
    51→		new ValidationPipe({ transform: true, whitelist: true, forbidNonWhitelisted: true }),
    52→	);
    53→
    54→	app.setGlobalPrefix(globalPrefix, {
    55→		exclude: ['liveness', 'readiness'],
    56→	});
    57→
    58→	setupSwagger({ app, fastifyAdapter, globalPrefix });
    59→
    60→	if (!isDev) {
    61→		app.enableShutdownHooks();
    62→	}
    63→
    64→	await app.listen(port, '0.0.0.0');
    65→
    66→	Logger.log(`Application running on port ${String(port)}`, 'Bootstrap');
    67→
    68→	return app;
    69→}
    70→
    71→let app: NestFastifyApplication | undefined;
    72→
    73→async function startApp(): Promise<void> {
    74→	try {
    75→		app = await bootstrap();
    76→	} catch (err: unknown) {
    77→		Logger.error('Failed to start application', err, 'Bootstrap');
    78→		process.exit(1);
    79→	}
    80→}
    81→
    82→void startApp();
    83→
    84→if (module.hot) {
    85→	module.hot.accept();
    86→	module.hot.dispose(() => {
    87→		void app?.close();
    88→	});
    89→}
    90→
```

```
All done. Here is a full summary:

---

## Summary

### Commands Run

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `npm run lint:fix` | Passed (0 errors, 13 pre-existing warnings) |
| `npm run format` | Passed (all files unchanged or reformatted cleanly) |
| `npm run type-check` | 1 pre-existing error in `test/jest.config.ts` (expected, not fixed) |
| `npm run test:unit` | 279/279 tests passed |

---

### Issues Found and Fixed

**`src/modules/session/session.service.ts`**
- 5 unused `catch (error)` variables across the Redis fallback try/catch blocks. Fixed by replacing with bare `catch` (TypeScript 2019+ feature).

**`src/modules/auth/guards/auth.guard.ts`**
- `request` was typed as `any`, causing 6 unsafe-access/unsafe-assignment errors. Fixed by importing `FastifyRequest` from `fastify` and typing the request as `FastifyRequest & { user?: IRequestUser }`.
- `extractTokenFromHeader(request: any)` violated the custom `require-object-params` rule and used `any`. Fixed by switching to object destructuring parameter `({ headers }: FastifyRequest)`.

**`src/modules/auth/guards/fastify-throttler.guard.ts`**
- `getRequestResponse` return type was `{ req: any; res: any }`. Fixed by importing `FastifyRequest` and `FastifyReply` from `fastify` and using them as the proper types.

**`src/modules/auth/guards/roles.guard.ts`**
- `request` was typed as `any`. Fixed by typing it as `FastifyRequest & { user?: IRequestUser }`.
- Unnecessary condition warning for `!requiredRoles` (since `getAllAndOverride` is typed as non-nullable by TypeScript, but at runtime returns `undefined` when no metadata is set). Kept the runtime guard and suppressed with `// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition`.

**`test/jest.coverage.config.ts`**
- Missing `import/no-dynamic-require` ESLint disable on the dynamic `require()` call. Fixed by adding it to the existing disable comment on that line.

**`src/unit-tests/env-validations.config.unit-spec.ts`**
- `validEnvConfig` only contained 7 fields but the `EnvironmentVariables` validation class requires 21 fields (after all new modules were added). Fixed by adding all required fields: `DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_URL_READ_REPLICA`, `REDIS_URL`, `REDIS_READ_URL`, `JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRY`, `JWT_REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS`, all OAuth fields, `FRONTEND_URL`, and `ENCRYPTION_KEY`.

---

### Pre-existing Warnings Left Intentionally

- **Migrations** (`up`/`down` functions): Knex API callbacks cannot use object destructuring - these warnings cannot be resolved without breaking Knex.
- **`fastify-throttler.guard.ts`** `getRequestResponse(context)`: Override of a base class method - signature cannot be changed.
- **`invitation.controller.ts` line 93**: Defensive `!currentUser` check that TypeScript considers unnecessary because `@CurrentUser()` guarantees a non-null value.
- **`oauth-account.model.ts` line 137**: Unnecessary condition on `access_token` null check.

> AGENT

No existing Docker setup. Clean slate. Current env files point to `localhost:5432` (PG) and `localhost:6379` (Redis) with no replica differentiation.

First question:

**Should the NestJS app itself run inside Docker, or just the infrastructure (PG, Redis)?**

Options:
- **A) Infra only** — PG + Redis in Docker, NestJS runs natively via `npm run start:dev`. Faster DX, easier debugging.
- **B) Full stack** — NestJS also in Docker. Consistent environment, but slower rebuilds and harder to debug.
- **C) Both** — `docker-compose.yml` for infra-only (dev default), separate `docker-compose.full.yml` that also includes the app.

> DEVELOPER

yes lets use app inside docker as well

> AGENT

**How should dev and test environments be separated?**

Options:
- **A) Single docker-compose with profiles** — `docker compose --profile dev up` vs `docker compose --profile test up`. Shared file, profiles control which containers start. Test containers use different ports.
- **B) Two separate compose files** — `docker-compose.yml` (dev) and `docker-compose.test.yml` (test). Fully independent, no risk of cross-contamination.
- **C) Base + override files** — `docker-compose.yml` (shared base) + `docker-compose.dev.yml` (dev overrides) + `docker-compose.test.yml` (test overrides). DRY but more complex.

My lean: **B** — simplest, cleanest separation, no profile confusion. Test env can be spun up/torn down independently without touching dev.

> DEVELOPER

i changed my mind...for previous question answer is Infra only

> AGENT

Good call — faster DX. Back to the separation question, same options apply. Which one?

- **A) Single docker-compose with profiles**
- **B) Two separate compose files** — `docker-compose.yml` (dev) + `docker-compose.test.yml` (test)
- **C) Base + override files**

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

Next question:

**PostgreSQL replication method for local dev?**

Options:
- **A) Streaming replication** — Real PG primary + standby with `pg_basebackup` and WAL streaming. Closest to production. More complex setup (init scripts, replication slots).
- **B) Two independent PG instances** — Primary on port 5432, "replica" on 5433. No actual replication — app just reads from the second instance. Simpler, but data diverges unless you seed both.
- **C) Single PG instance, two connection strings** — One PG container, app connects with two URLs pointing to the same instance. Simplest. Tests the connection separation logic without actual replication overhead.

My lean: **C** for dev (no need for real replication locally — you're testing app logic, not PG replication). **A** is overkill for local dev. **B** causes data sync headaches.

For test environment: same question applies — do you want real replicas there too, or is single-instance-two-URLs sufficient?

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

For both dev and test, or just dev?

- **A) Streaming replication for both dev and test**
- **B) Streaming replication for dev, single instance for test** (tests run faster)

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

Next question:

**Redis replication — same approach?**

Options:
- **A) Real Redis replication** — Primary + replica using `replicaof`. Redis native replication is trivial to set up compared to PG.
- **B) Single Redis, two URLs** — Same instance, two connection strings.

My lean: **A** — Redis replication setup is literally one config line (`replicaof redis-primary 6379`), so no reason not to.

> DEVELOPER

A

> AGENT

Last question:

**Port mapping strategy for dev vs test profiles?**

Options:
- **A) Different host ports** — Dev: PG 5432/5433, Redis 6379/6380. Test: PG 5442/5443, Redis 6389/6390.
- **B) No host port mapping for test** — Test containers communicate via Docker network only. Tests run inside Docker network (via `docker compose exec`) or the app connects via container names. No port conflicts possible.
- **C) Same ports, different compose projects** — Use `--project-name` to isolate. Both use 5432/6379 but in different Docker networks.

My lean: **A** — explicit, no ambiguity, both environments accessible from host simultaneously. You can run dev and test in parallel without conflicts.

Perfect! Now let me create a comprehensive summary of all the Docker and Redis infrastructure patterns found:

## Docker and Redis Infrastructure Patterns - Recommendations Service

### **1. Docker Files Found**

#### Main Production Dockerfile
**Path**: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/Dockerfile`
- Multi-stage build (build → dist)
- Uses AWS ECR image: `916869144969.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/customink/nodejs:jammy-24`
- GitHub Packages authentication via Docker secrets
- Production build with NestJS compilation
- Exports port 8080
- Health check: `curl -f http://localhost:8080/is_it_up` (30s interval, 10s timeout, 3 retries, 30s start-period)
- Includes executable scripts for cron jobs (cron-trending-items, cron-sync-source-styles, cron-populate-style-copurchases)
- Runs with `npm run start:prod` command

#### Dev Container Dockerfile
**Path**: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.devcontainer/local/Dockerfile`
- Base: `node:24.13-bullseye`
- Installs git, curl, wget, vim, ssh
- Configures Docker socket access and creates docker group
- Runs as node user

### **2. Docker Compose Configuration**

**Path**: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.devcontainer/local/docker-compose.yml`

#### Service: recommendations-service
- Builds from context with dev Dockerfile
- Mounts workspaces and node_modules volume
- Docker socket and SSH auth socket access
- **Redis environment variables**:
  - `REDIS_URL: redis://redis-write:6379`
  - `REDIS_READ_URL: redis://redis-read:6379`
- Database URLs (MySQL write/read replicas)
- Traefik routing: `Host(recommendations.customink.d3v)` on port 9009
- Depends on Redis and MySQL services with health checks

#### Service: redis-write
- Image: `redis:7.0-alpine`
- Container name: `redis-write`
- Port mapping: `6377:6379` (localhost:6377)
- Command: `redis-server --replicaof no one` (master)
- Restart policy: `unless-stopped`
- Health check: `redis-cli ping` (10s interval, 5s timeout, 5 retries)

#### Service: redis-read
- Image: `redis:7.0-alpine`
- Container name: `redis-read`
- Port mapping: `6378:6379` (localhost:6378)
- Command: `redis-server --replicaof redis-write 6379` (replica)
- Restart policy: `unless-stopped`
- Depends on redis-write with health condition
- Health check: `redis-cli ping` (10s interval, 5s timeout, 5 retries)

#### Service: mysql-write
- Image: `mysql:8.0.30`
- Port: `3336:3306`
- Configuration: Binary logging, ROW format, GTID enabled
- Databases: `recommendations`, `recommendations_test`
- Health check: mysqladmin ping (10s interval, 20s timeout, 10 retries)

#### Service: mysql-read
- Image: `mysql:8.0.30`
- Port: `3337:3306`
- Read-only mode, relay logs enabled
- Health check: mysqladmin ping (10s interval, 20s timeout, 10 retries)

### **3. Redis Configuration Pattern**

**From Environment Variables** (`.env.example` and `.env`):
```
REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6377
REDIS_READ_URL=redis://localhost:6378
```

**Dependencies in package.json**:
- `ioredis` (^5.10.0) - Redis client
- `@keyv/redis` (^5.1.6) - Keyv Redis adapter
- `redlock` - Distributed locking

### **4. MySQL Replication Configuration**

#### Write Node Config (`.devcontainer/local/mysql/write/write.cnf`)
```
server-id = 1
log-bin = mysql-bin
binlog-format = ROW
binlog-do-db = recommendations, recommendations_test
gtid_mode = ON
enforce_gtid_consistency = ON
innodb_buffer_pool_size = 256M
innodb_log_file_size = 64M
expire_logs_days = 7
max_binlog_size = 100M
```

#### Read Node Config (`.devcontainer/local/mysql/read/read.cnf`)
```
server-id = 2
relay-log = mysql-relay-log
log-slave-updates = 1
read-only = 1
replicate-do-db = recommendations, recommendations_test
gtid_mode = ON
slave-skip-errors = 1062
```

### **5. Database Setup Scripts**

**Path**: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.devcontainer/local/`

Files:
- `setup-replication.sh` - Configures MySQL write/read replication, creates users and databases, tests replication
- `setup-all.sh` - Orchestrates complete database setup (replication → migrations for dev → migrations for test)
- `postCreate` - Post-container creation hook (npm install, .env setup, script permissions)
- `verify-setup.sh` - Verification script

### **6. Dev Container Configuration**

**Path**: `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.devcontainer/local/devcontainer.json`
- Service: `recommendations-service`
- Workspace: `/workspaces/recommendations-service`
- Features: GitHub CLI, apt packages (vim, iputils-ping, zsh, ssh, tmux, default-mysql-client)
- VS Code extensions: ESLint, Prettier, Docker, Jest Runner
- postCreateCommand: `./.devcontainer/local/postCreate`
- postStartCommand: `./.devcontainer/local/setup-all.sh`

### **7. Application Database Access Pattern**

**From `.env` Configuration**:
```
DATABASE_URL=mysql://app_user:app_password@localhost:3336/recommendations
DATABASE_URL_READ_REPLICA=mysql://app_user:app_password@localhost:3337/recommendations
```

**Users Created**:
- `replica_user` - Replication privileged user
- `app_user` - Application user (ALL privileges on write, SELECT on read)

### **8. Port Mapping Summary**

| Service | Port | Purpose |
|---------|------|---------|
| Application | 9009 | NestJS API (Traefik routed to recommendations.customink.d3v) |
| Redis Write | 6377 | Cache master |
| Redis Read | 6378 | Cache replica |
| MySQL Write | 3336 | Database master |
| MySQL Read | 3337 | Database replica |

### **9. Health Checks & Dependencies**

All services have health checks configured. The application depends on:
- `redis-write` (service_healthy)
- `redis-read` (service_healthy)
- `mysql-write` (service_healthy)
- `mysql-read` (service_healthy)

### **10. Network Configuration**

- Default network: `devcontainers` (external, shared)
- MySQL network: `mysql-network` (bridge driver, isolated)

### **11. Infrastructure-Related Scripts in `/bin`**

- `cron-populate-style-copurchases`
- `cron-sync-source-styles`
- `cron-trending-items`
- Other non-infrastructure scripts (bootstrap, build, server, test, lint, type-check, etc.)

### **12. Technology Stack**

- **Redis**: ioredis + @keyv/redis + redlock
- **MySQL**: mysql2 + Knex.js + nest-knexjs
- **AWS**: SDK (SQS), Redshift
- **Docker**: Multi-stage builds, Alpine images
- **Orchestration**: Docker Compose for local dev

> AGENT

All questions answered. Let me present the design section by section.

---

## Section 1: Container Architecture

```
docker-compose.yml (single file, profile-based)

Profile: dev (default)
┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐
│  pg-dev-write   │  │  pg-dev-read    │
│  PostgreSQL 16  │──│  Streaming      │
│  Port: 5432     │  │  Replica        │
│  DB: noname_dev │  │  Port: 5433     │
└─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘
┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐
│ redis-dev-write │  │ redis-dev-read  │
│ Redis 7-alpine  │──│ replicaof       │
│ Port: 6379      │  │ Port: 6380      │
└─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘

Profile: test
┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐
│ pg-test-write   │  │ pg-test-read    │
│ PostgreSQL 16   │──│ Streaming       │
│ Port: 5442      │  │ Replica         │
│ DB: noname_test │  │ Port: 5443      │
└─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘
┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐
│ redis-test-write│  │ redis-test-read │
│ Redis 7-alpine  │──│ replicaof       │
│ Port: 6389      │  │ Port: 6390      │
└─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘
```

**Port mapping:**

| Service | Dev Port | Test Port |
|---------|----------|-----------|
| PG Write | 5432 | 5442 |
| PG Read | 5433 | 5443 |
| Redis Write | 6379 | 6389 |
| Redis Read | 6380 | 6390 |

**Usage:**
```bash
docker compose --profile dev up -d     # start dev infra
docker compose --profile test up -d    # start test infra
docker compose --profile dev --profile test up -d  # both
```

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

bro lets have single profile, but just test DB with suffix name name_test... the same for redis

> AGENT

Much simpler. Single set of containers, two databases on the same PG instance, Redis namespaced by the app.

## Section 1: Container Architecture (Revised)

```
docker-compose.yml (no profiles needed)

┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐
│  pg-write        │  │  pg-read         │
│  PostgreSQL 16   │──│  Streaming       │
│  Port: 5432      │  │  Replica         │
│  DBs: noname_dev │  │  Port: 5433      │
│       noname_test│  │                  │
└──────────────────┘  └──────────────────┘
┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐
│  redis-write     │  │  redis-read      │
│  Redis 7-alpine  │──│  replicaof       │
│  Port: 6379      │  │  Port: 6380      │
└──────────────────┘  └──────────────────┘
```

**4 containers total.** PG primary creates both `noname_dev` and `noname_test` databases. Redis is shared — dev and test are isolated by key namespace (already handled by `${SERVER_NAME}:${NODE_ENV}` prefix in the cache module).

**Env files point to:**
- `.env`: `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_dev`
- `.env.test`: `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_test`
- Both use same Redis URLs, different namespaces via `NODE_ENV`

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

lets try

> AGENT

## Section 2: PostgreSQL Streaming Replication Setup

**PG Write (primary):**
- `postgresql.conf`: enable WAL (`wal_level = replica`, `max_wal_senders = 3`)
- `pg_hba.conf`: allow replication connections from the read container
- Init script: creates `noname_dev` + `noname_test` databases, `app_user` (app), `replication_user` (replication)

**PG Read (replica):**
- Starts with `pg_basebackup` from primary
- `primary_conninfo` points to pg-write
- Read-only by default (hot standby)

**Init flow:**
1. pg-write starts, runs init scripts (create DBs, users)
2. pg-read waits for pg-write health check
3. pg-read runs `pg_basebackup` to copy data
4. pg-read connects as streaming replica

**Files needed:**
```
docker/
├── docker-compose.yml
├── postgres/
│   ├── primary/
│   │   ├── postgresql.conf
│   │   ├── pg_hba.conf
│   │   └── init-db.sh          # Create DBs + users
│   └── replica/
│       └── start-replica.sh    # pg_basebackup + start standby
└── scripts/
    └── setup.sh                # One-command setup
```

Redis needs zero config files — just `--replicaof redis-write 6379` as the command.

Look right?

}

{

  "content": "#!/bin/bash\nset -e\n\necho \"Waiting for primary to be ready...\"\nuntil pg_isready -h pg-write -p 5432 -U postgres; do\n    echo \"Primary not ready yet, retrying in 2s...\"\n    sleep 2\ndone\n\necho \"Primary is ready. Cleaning data directory...\"\nrm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\n\necho \"Running pg_basebackup...\"\nPGPASSWORD=[REDACTED] pg_basebackup \\\n    -h pg-write \\\n    -D /var/lib/postgresql/data \\\n    -U replication_user \\\n    -Fp \\\n    -Xs \\\n    -P \\\n    -R\n\necho \"Configuring replica...\"\ncat >> /var/lib/postgresql/data/postgresql.conf <<EOF\nhot_standby = on\nprimary_conninfo = 'host=pg-write port=5432 user=replication_user password=[REDACTED]'\nEOF\n\necho \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nexec postgres\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docker/scripts/setup.sh",
  "content": "#!/bin/bash\nset -e\n\nSCRIPT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")\" && pwd)\"\nPROJECT_DIR=\"$(dirname \"$SCRIPT_DIR\")\"\n\necho \"=== Starting infrastructure ===\"\ndocker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" up -d\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Waiting for services to be healthy ===\"\n\necho -n \"Waiting for pg-write...\"\nuntil docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T pg-write pg_isready -U postgres > /dev/null 2>&1; do\n    echo -n \".\"\n    sleep 1\ndone\necho \" ready\"\n\necho -n \"Waiting for pg-read...\"\nuntil docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T pg-read pg_isready -U postgres > /dev/null 2>&1; do\n    echo -n \".\"\n    sleep 1\ndone\necho \" ready\"\n\necho -n \"Waiting for redis-write...\"\nuntil docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T redis-write redis-cli ping > /dev/null 2>&1; do\n    echo -n \".\"\n    sleep 1\ndone\necho \" ready\"\n\necho -n \"Waiting for redis-read...\"\nuntil docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T redis-read redis-cli ping > /dev/null 2>&1; do\n    echo -n \".\"\n    sleep 1\ndone\necho \" ready\"\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Verifying PostgreSQL replication ===\"\nREPLICATION_STATUS=$(docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T pg-write psql -U postgres -c \"SELECT client_addr, state FROM pg_stat_replication;\" 2>/dev/null)\necho \"$REPLICATION_STATUS\"\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Verifying Redis replication ===\"\nREDIS_STATUS=$(docker compose -f \"$PROJECT_DIR/docker-compose.yml\" exec -T redis-read redis-cli info replication 2>/dev/null | grep role)\necho \"Redis read role: $REDIS_STATUS\"\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Running migrations (dev) ===\"\ncd \"$PROJECT_DIR/backend\"\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_dev npx knex migrate:latest --knexfile src/knexfile.ts 2>/dev/null || echo \"Dev migrations skipped (no migrations or knexfile issue)\"\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Running migrations (test) ===\"\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_test npx knex migrate:latest --knexfile src/knexfile.ts 2>/dev/null || echo \"Test migrations skipped (no migrations or knexfile issue)\"\n\necho \"\"\necho \"=== Infrastructure ready ===\"\necho \"\"\necho \"Dev DB:    postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_dev\"\necho \"Dev DB RR: postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5433/noname_dev\"\necho \"Test DB:   postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5432/noname_test\"\necho \"Test DB RR:postgresql://app_user:app_password@localhost:5433/noname_test\"\necho \"Redis W:   redis://localhost:6379\"\necho \"Redis R:   redis://localhost:6380\"\n"
}

> AGENT

Done. To start everything:

```bash
npm run infra:setup    # full setup (start + wait + verify + migrations)
# or
npm run infra:up       # just start containers
npm run infra:reset    # nuke volumes and restart fresh
```

**Containers:**

| Container | Port | Purpose |
|-----------|------|---------|
| noname-pg-write | 5432 | PostgreSQL primary |
| noname-pg-read | 5433 | PostgreSQL streaming replica |
| noname-redis-write | 6379 | Redis primary |
| noname-redis-read | 6380 | Redis replica |

> AGENT

OrbStack's VM can't reach Docker Hub — the host internet works but OrbStack's internal networking is timing out.

Fix: restart OrbStack.

```bash
! orb restart
```

Or from the OrbStack menu bar icon → Restart. Then retry `docker compose up -d`.

Alternatively, if images are already cached locally:

# AUTO-GENERATED BY ORBSTACK. DO NOT EDIT.
# To make changes, add or override hosts at the top of ~/.ssh/config

Host orb
  Hostname 127.0.0.1
  Port 32222
  # SSH user syntax:
  #   <container>@orb to connect to <container> as the default user (matching your macOS user)
  #   <user>@<container>@orb to connect to <container> as <user>
  # Examples:
  #   ubuntu@orb: container "ubuntu", user matching your macOS user
  #   root@fedora@orb: container "fedora", user "root"
  User default
  # replace or symlink ~/.orbstack/ssh/id_ed25519 file to change the key
  IdentityFile ~/.orbstack/ssh/id_ed25519
  # only use this key

> DEVELOPER

how to fix it?

> AGENT

The script runs as root. Fix: use `su-exec` (alpine's `gosu`) to drop to the `postgres` user before starting:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01PXVSVFr1bJ6LMhGCtk7ps9
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docker/postgres/replica/start-replica.sh",
  "old_string": "echo \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nexec postgres",
  "new_string": "echo \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data\nexec su-exec postgres postgres"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01PXVSVFr1bJ6LMhGCtk7ps9

```

{

  "old_string": "echo \"Primary is ready. Cleaning data directory...\"\nrm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\n\necho \"Running pg_basebackup...\"\nPGPASSWORD=[REDACTED] pg_basebackup \\\n    -h pg-write \\\n    -D /var/lib/postgresql/data \\\n    -U replication_user \\\n    -Fp \\\n    -Xs \\\n    -P \\\n    -R\n\necho \"Configuring replica...\"\ncat >> /var/lib/postgresql/data/postgresql.conf <<EOF\nhot_standby = on\nprimary_conninfo = 'host=pg-write port=5432 user=replication_user password=[REDACTED]'\nEOF\n\necho \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data\nexec su-exec postgres postgres",
  "new_string": "echo \"Primary is ready. Cleaning data directory...\"\nrm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\nmkdir -p /var/lib/postgresql/data\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data\n\necho \"Running pg_basebackup...\"\nsu-exec postgres bash -c \"PGPASSWORD=[REDACTED] pg_basebackup \\\n    -h pg-write \\\n    -D /var/lib/postgresql/data \\\n    -U replication_user \\\n    -Fp \\\n    -Xs \\\n    -P \\\n    -R\"\n\necho \"Configuring replica...\"\ncat >> /var/lib/postgresql/data/postgresql.conf <<EOF\nhot_standby = on\nprimary_conninfo = 'host=pg-write port=5432 user=replication_user password=[REDACTED]'\nEOF\n\necho \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nexec su-exec postgres postgres"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docker/postgres/replica/start-replica.sh",
  "content": "#!/bin/bash\nset -e\n\necho \"Waiting for primary to be ready...\"\nuntil pg_isready -h pg-write -p 5432 -U postgres; do\n    echo \"Primary not ready yet, retrying in 2s...\"\n    sleep 2\ndone\n\necho \"Primary is ready. Cleaning data directory...\"\nrm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\nmkdir -p /var/lib/postgresql/data\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data\n\necho \"Running pg_basebackup...\"\ngosu postgres bash -c \"PGPASSWORD=[REDACTED] pg_basebackup \\\n    -h pg-write \\\n    -D /var/lib/postgresql/data \\\n    -U replication_user \\\n    -Fp \\\n    -Xs \\\n    -P \\\n    -R\"\n\necho \"Configuring replica...\"\ncat >> /var/lib/postgresql/data/postgresql.conf <<EOF\nhot_standby = on\nprimary_conninfo = 'host=pg-write port=5432 user=replication_user password=[REDACTED]'\nEOF\n\necho \"Starting PostgreSQL replica...\"\nexec gosu postgres postgres\n"
}

> AGENT

Need to set `chmod 700` on the data dir. `pg_basebackup` requires it:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01VDomwJtpavteSXdUh3o1sc
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/docker/postgres/replica/start-replica.sh",
  "old_string": "rm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\nmkdir -p /var/lib/postgresql/data\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data",
  "new_string": "rm -rf /var/lib/postgresql/data/*\nmkdir -p /var/lib/postgresql/data\nchmod 700 /var/lib/postgresql/data\nchown -R postgres:postgres /var/lib/postgresql/data"
}
```

All tests pass and the commit is created. Here's a summary of what was done:

**Files modified in source code (required fixes):**

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/utils/encryption.util.ts` — Added `Buffer.from(key, 'hex')` to decode the 64-char hex encryption key to 32 bytes before passing to `createCipheriv`/`createDecipheriv`
- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/auth/auth.controller.ts` — Added explicit `.status(HttpStatus.FOUND)` before each `.redirect()` call to fix Fastify v5 behavior (it respects any previously-set status code instead of defaulting to 302)
- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/modules/auth/guards/auth.guard.ts` — Added `tryPopulateUser` private method so public routes still populate `request.user` when a valid JWT is present (required for the invitation accept endpoint)

**Files modified in tests:**

- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/unit-tests/encryption.util.unit-spec.ts` — Updated test key to `'a'.repeat(64)` (valid 64-char hex string) to match the new hex-decode behavior
- `/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/no-name-proj/backend/src/unit-tests/auth.controller.unit-spec.ts` — Added `status` mock to `mockReply` and updated assertions to check `reply.status(302)` chaining
- All four E2E spec files — Fixed `process.env['DATABASE_URL'] as string` for TypeScript strict null check, fixed import ordering, and corrected status code expectations (201 for `POST /api/users/me/company`)

**Root cause summary:** Three separate bugs were discovered and fixed — a hex encoding mismatch in the encryption utility, a Fastify v5 redirect status behavior change, and a missing "optional auth" pattern in the auth guard for public routes that also need user context.

2. **Fastify redirects** — Fastify v5 requires explicit `.status(302)` before `.redirect()`
3. **Auth guard** — Public routes with optional auth (like invitation accept) weren't populating `request.user` even when a valid token was present
